Contrarian Takes

Executive Summary

  • Voice Agent / AI Receptionist is the easiest first sell. $297-500/mo, immediate ROI (missed calls = $3-5K lost per med spa/HVAC). Zack Kirk: $85K/mo, Lee Blue: $100K/mo on this alone.
  • Workflow Automation is highest-volume. n8n/Make.com at $1,500-5K/build. Nick Saraev: $72K/mo with "boring" automations. Target B2B/digital, NOT local.
  • Lead Reactivation is the easiest ROI pitch. "You paid for these leads. They sit in a spreadsheet." $1-5K setup, pure profit.
  • Realistic revenue: $10-15K/mo in 3-6 months. Zubair: "18-year-olds claiming $300K/month are lying."
  • GoHighLevel dominates as the platform. White-label AI on GHL = 10x markup, zero custom dev.
  • Daniel Fazio's counter: "95-99% of AI agencies fail in 90 days. Sell proven marketing services, use AI to deliver 90% cheaper."
  • Client acquisition: Cold call + free demo most common. Facebook groups + AI content (Lee Blue: 1 post = 80 calls, 6 closes = $9,500 MRR).

Unknown (HighLevel affiliate creator)
How To Get More Leads and Booked Appointments Usin		 Voice Agent Teaching agency owners how to sell AI voice receptionist services using HighLeve Local businesses missing inbound calls - real estate investo Not explicitly stated for setup $300/month suggested selling price Recently sold to two clients. Shows 10 qualif Demo-based selling - show real examples of AI hand

Full Description

Teaching agency owners how to sell AI voice receptionist services using HighLevel Voice AI feature. The system answers inbound calls, qualifies leads, and books appointments automatically. In 23 days of testing, got 10 qualified calls with conversations averaging 5-7 minutes each. Provides a calculator to demonstrate ROI to business owners, a copy-paste prompt template, and backend automations. Charges clients $300/month for the service with minimal ongoing fulfillment.

Pain Point

Businesses miss inbound calls and lose potential customers. If a business misses 10 calls per month and each job is worth $1,000, even converting 30% of missed calls into appointments means $1,000+ extra revenue per month. For real estate investors, one deal at $10,000 makes the entire system worthwhile.

Pricing Justification

ROI calculator shows: if business misses 10 calls/month, average job $1,000, 30% conversion rate = $1,000+ extra revenue/month. Even worst case ($500 jobs, 10% conversion) = still positive ROI against $300/month fee.

Delivery Process

1. Get HighLevel account. 2. Click into AI agents → Voice AI → Create custom agent. 3. Name the agent (uses Jackie). 4. Input business name and details. 5. Copy-paste the provided prompt template. 6. Adjust prompt for specific niche. 7. Set up backend automations (simple and advanced versions). 8. Connect to clients phone system.

Tools Stack

HighLevel (primary platform - Voice AI feature, CRM, automations), HighLevel affiliate program for partner revenue. Backend automations built within HighLevel for lead routing and appointment booking.

Client Acquisition

Demo-based selling - show real examples of AI handling calls, then use ROI calculator to demonstrate math. Leverage affiliate program for social proof. Recommend linking to clients existing advertising sources (Google Ads, Facebook Ads, direct mail).

Outreach Script

N/A - teaches demo-based selling approach rather than specific outreach scripts

Sales Process

1. Show real example of AI handling calls (play recordings - they only need to hear 45 seconds). 2. Overcome main hurdle: will people actually talk to the AI? Show proof they do. 3. Use ROI calculator to demonstrate math with their specific numbers. 4. Suggest integrating with existing marketing channels. 5. Close on monthly retainer.

Objection Handling

Main objection: Will people actually talk to an AI? Response: Dont argue theoretically - show real call recordings proving people engage in 5-7 minute conversations. It doesnt matter what you think or what I think - people ARE calling this and going through the system.

Contrarian Insight

Dont sell AI receptionist on time-saving alone - that is not a strong enough value proposition unless the owner personally answers phones. Instead lean into the money-making angle: missed calls = missed revenue. Position the AI as a revenue generation tool, not a convenience tool.

Mistake Warning

Trying to have prospects call the AI themselves to evaluate it. They will pick apart how it sounds or doubt they would personally go through the system. Instead, show REAL results - actual call recordings with real leads who engaged and booked appointments.

Scalability

Set up once per client, system runs automatically. No ongoing fulfillment required (ads, content creation, etc). Can link to multiple advertising sources per client to increase call volume. Stack multiple clients at $300/month each.

Best Quotes

The number one reason is because you essentially can set this up once and the system will continue to work and it produces actual value to the business owner.
I dont think saving them time is a huge value proposition unless theyre literally answering the phones themselves. I prefer to lean into the actual money making.
The main hurdle to jump over is like will people actually talk to the AI. They dont really care how it sounds. Even if its automated.
It doesnt really matter what you think or what I think. People are calling this and going through the system.

Action Steps

  • Build ROI calculator spreadsheet showing missed calls × average deal value × conversion rate to quantify the opportunity for each prospect
  • Set up a HighLevel Voice AI agent with the provided prompt template and test on your own business first to get real call recordings
  • Sell the service at $300/month positioned as revenue generation (not time saving) - even one converted call can justify the fee
  • Recommend clients integrate ALL marketing channels through the AI receptionist to maximize call volume and ROI

Michele Torti
$10k/mo AI Agency from Scratch		 Voice Agent Michele Torti's step-by-step framework for building an AI agency from scratch in Local businesses running ads, any business with phone-based Implementation fee: $2,600-$3,200 (cost-saving) or $9,600 (revenue-uplift), calculated from value formulas Retainer for ongoing monitoring and optimization Scaled to 6 figures working with 50+ business Not detailed extensively - mentions proven results

Full Description

Michele Torti's step-by-step framework for building an AI agency from scratch in 2026, specifically focused on voice agents. Three-service stack: inbound receptionist (24/7 answering), speed to lead (call within 5 mins of ad opt-in), and lead reactivation (call old database leads). Main offer is receptionist, upsells are speed-to-lead and reactivation. Value-based pricing using cost-saving and revenue-uplift formulas.

Pain Point

Businesses miss calls after hours = missed revenue. Leads not followed up within 5 minutes (100x less likely to convert). Old leads sitting in CRM untouched. No manpower for consistent follow-up.

Pricing Justification

Tangible math behind every price. Cost-saving: replacing hours of human work. Revenue-uplift: percentage of revenue the system generates. Never charge based on time - charge based on value.

Delivery Process

1. Choose voice agents as AI opportunity 2. Learn Retell AI + n8n on YouTube 3. Build inbound receptionist (24/7, filter spam, book appointments, transfer calls) 4. Add speed to lead (Facebook ad opt-in -> AI calls within 5 min) 5. Add lead reactivation (call old database contacts) 6. Value-based pricing per system 7. Deliver and retain

Tools Stack

Retell AI (voice agent platform), n8n (workflow automation), YouTube (free learning), Facebook ads integration for speed-to-lead

Client Acquisition

Not detailed extensively - mentions proven results with clients as credibility. Six-month one-on-one program available.

Sales Process

Not detailed in first 15K chars

Objection Handling

Not detailed in first 15K chars. Framework emphasizes tangible math behind pricing eliminates 'pricing out of your ass.'

Contrarian Insight

Don't sell 'a voice agent' - businesses have no idea what that means. Sell the outcome (missed calls answered, leads followed up in 5 minutes, old leads reactivated). Three services for one client increases LTV dramatically vs finding new clients. Value-based pricing means you can charge $9.6K for something that takes hours to build. Feed is full of voice agents but it's algorithm, not saturation.

Mistake Warning

Charging $200-300/month recurring. Pricing based on time instead of value. Trying to sell 'voice agents' instead of outcomes. Being everything to everyone. Spending months in tutorial hell watching videos instead of picking one path and executing.

Scalability

Learn Retell + n8n -> Build inbound receptionist -> Upsell speed-to-lead -> Upsell reactivation -> Value-based pricing -> 3 services per client -> Scale

Best Quotes

If you're everything to everyone, you're nothing to no one
I used to charge based on the time. But if someone is better at doing a certain thing, why should they get paid less?
Leads followed up within 5 minutes are 100 times more likely to convert
Your whole feed is filled with voice agents. And by the way, it's the algorithm, it's not saturation

Action Steps

  • Learn Retell AI basics via YouTube tutorials this week
  • Build one inbound receptionist demo for a local business vertical
  • Calculate cost-saving and revenue-uplift pricing for your demo
  • Identify 10 businesses running Facebook ads that would benefit from speed-to-lead
  • Create a three-service proposal template (receptionist + speed-to-lead + reactivation)

Liam Ottley
$0 to $100K with AI		 Automation Liam Ottley interviews Michael Kelly who went from zero to $100K using the AI au Anyone starting from zero, especially young people with no t Started free for first 3 clients, then gradually increased Not specified - graduated pricing as skills improved $100K milestone reached. Five key milestones Warm network outreach (text messages to contacts,

Full Description

Liam Ottley interviews Michael Kelly who went from zero to $100K using the AI automation agency model. Five key milestones covered: learning AI tools, getting first clients (free), scaling to paid clients, building systems, and reaching $100K. Michael specialized in workflow automations using make.com, started with zero dollars, and got first clients through warm network outreach.

Pain Point

Overwhelm from too many AI tools. Analysis paralysis. Don't know what to focus on. No clients, no case studies, no money to start. Need momentum and quick wins early.

Pricing Justification

Working free builds experience, case studies, and momentum. Quick wins keep you motivated. Investment in fundamentals pays off long-term.

Delivery Process

1. Learn one tool deeply (make.com for workflow automations) 2. Reach out to warm network 3. Work free for first 3 clients 4. Build portfolio and case studies 5. Gradually increase prices 6. Scale through referrals and community

Tools Stack

make.com (primary - workflow automations), VoiceFlow (voice agents), Lovable (vibe coding). Core skill: understanding APIs and how apps talk to each other.

Client Acquisition

Warm network outreach (text messages to contacts, second-degree connections - sister's boss, friend's dad). School community posting. LinkedIn content.

Outreach Script

Warm outreach text: 'Hey, I just wanted to help you out. Can you help on a call?' For no network: 'Do you happen to know anybody who'd be interested in help with [AI automation]?' Sent 6 days after first seeing automation videos.

Sales Process

1. Text warm contacts 2. Offer free help 3. Get on call 4. Deliver value 5. Ask for referrals 6. Use School community for additional leads 7. Build LinkedIn presence 8. Graduate to paid

Objection Handling

I know no one: Dig deeper - sister's boss, friend's dad, second-degree connections. Your sister's friend's dad is a business owner. No money to start: YouTube is free, tools have free tiers. No technical knowledge: APIs are the fundamental skill, learnable on YouTube.

Contrarian Insight

The fundamental skill is NOT make.com or any specific tool - it's understanding APIs and how software talks to each other. That transfers across everything. Work free for first clients even if you're 45 with responsibilities - it's doing the unscalable before you scale. Niche down within AI automation to one subset. Focus beats breadth.

Mistake Warning

Trying to learn all AI tools at once. Being a generalist instead of specializing. Not reaching out to warm network because you think you don't know anyone. Overthinking the outreach message. Not sending that first message within days of learning.

Scalability

Learn one tool -> Warm outreach -> Free clients -> Build skills -> Community presence -> Paid clients -> LinkedIn content -> Scale

Best Quotes

I asked my sister to give me 80% of what she had and my dad as well and I put it onto the accelerator and I went full in
I had zero dollars. I literally had zero idea what anything was. Just send a message because why not?
The fundamental skill here is APIs, understanding how apps talk to each other
Once you dig deep into it you find that your sister's boss is a restaurant owner

Action Steps

  • Pick ONE tool (make.com, VoiceFlow, or Lovable) and learn it this week via YouTube
  • Go through phone contacts and identify 10 people to reach out to
  • Send warm outreach messages to 5 people today
  • Offer to build something free for the first 3 people who respond
  • Join a free AI automation community (School, Discord) for support
Nick Saraev
Why I Dont Sell AI to Local Businesses		 Web Build Build AI-powered websites for local service businesses with no online presence u Local home service businesses without websites: plumbers, el 1000-5000 one-time depending on complexity Not explicit but automation management creates opportunity 2000-3000 per client. Rose Cleaning Co at 300 Google Maps prospecting. Build free mockup BEFORE

Full Description

Build AI-powered websites for local service businesses with no online presence using GoHighLevel template-based software. Find businesses on Google Maps with no website, customize a professional template in 10-20 minutes, then pitch the pre-built website for free as foot-in-the-door. Once they love it, charge 1K-5K to set it live. Add backend follow-up automations to nurture captured leads. Zero coding required.

Pain Point

Businesses losing thousands per month because customers search Google, see no website, move to competitor. Service owners great at craft but terrible at marketing.

Pricing Justification

Website ranks on Google = more leads = more revenue. Professional presence builds trust.

Delivery Process

1. Search Google Maps. 2. ID businesses with no website. 3. Save contacts. 4. Open GHL Sites. 5. Find template. 6. Customize 10-20 min. 7. Add automations. 8. Reach out. 9. Get reply first. 10. Zoom call. 11. Set live, collect payment.

Tools Stack

GoHighLevel - builder/templates/automations (30-day trial). Google Maps - prospecting. Google Sheets - organize. Zoom - calls.

Client Acquisition

Google Maps prospecting. Build free mockup BEFORE outreach. Phone/email/text scripts.

Outreach Script

PHONE: Hey is this the owner? I specialize in helping local businesses get more leads using a smart AI website. I built you a free mockup - no cost no strings. What email to send it to? EMAIL Subject: Free Website for [Company]. Body: I put together a free homepage sample. Reply and I will send it. TEXT: I built you a free sample for [business]. Want me to send it over?

Sales Process

1. Find on Maps. 2. Build free mockup. 3. Outreach. 4. Get REPLY (first yes). 5. Send mockup. 6. Zoom. 7. Set live today for 1500-5000. 8. Collect payment.

Objection Handling

Free mockup removes risk. Get reply before sending link. Simple lead gen website pitch.

Contrarian Insight

Give websites away free first then charge to set live. Sale after they love it. Reply more important than link. No tech skills needed.

Mistake Warning

Sending link without getting reply first. They glance and forget. Reply creates commitment.

Scalability

Stack add-ons: automations, SEO, Google Maps optimization.

Best Quotes

Most local businesses are losing thousands just because they do not have a real website.
They are great at their craft but not very good with marketing and sales.
You want them to reply - that is their first yes.
Why would they say no to a free website?

Action Steps

  • Week 1: Google Maps 3 categories, ID 20 businesses, save in Sheet.
  • Week 2: GHL trial, build 5 mockups at 10-20 min each.
  • Week 3: Outreach all 20 via phone+email+text.
  • Week 4: Close 2-3 at 1500-3000. Add automations. Collect testimonials.
Unknown (Gym Members AI Agency)
Live Sales Call Breakdown - Closing AI Automation 		Voice Agent Live sales call breakdown showing how to close AI automation agency clients. Sol Local gym owners, fitness businesses. Broader: any local bus Low-ticket AI agent as foot-in-the-door (exact price not stated in first 12K chars) Upsold to $8,000 paid in full package, then another $8,000 - total $16,000+ in under 6 months $16 million in sales, $6 million in profits o Inbound sales calls. Prospects book calls after se

Full Description

Live sales call breakdown showing how to close AI automation agency clients. Sold a gym owner on a low-ticket AI agent for generating reviews and referrals, which then led to upsells totaling $16,000+ in less than 6 months. The sales process follows: introduction, discovery (diagnose problem with specific numbers), build doubt around current solution, identify goals, transition to expert positioning, tailored pitch, temperature check, price pitch, and close. Agency has generated over $16 million in sales and $6 million in profits over 4 years.

Pain Point

Gym owner struggling to grow membership after personal setbacks (COVID, illness). Currently at 40 members. Using Facebook ads through another agency (Big Little Gems) at $50-80 per acquisition. No review generation process despite claiming to have one - zero new reviews in 3 months. Missing foundational systems for reviews and referrals.

Pricing Justification

AI agent for reviews/referrals is differentiated from what competitor agencies offer. No other agency in the market is selling this specific solution, allowing foot-in-the-door even when client works with competitor.

Delivery Process

1. Discovery call diagnoses specific problem with numbers. 2. Build doubt around current solution. 3. Position yourself as expert with different approach. 4. Pitch tailored AI agent solution. 5. Temperature check before price reveal. 6. Price pitch and close. 7. Team upsells additional services post-close.

Tools Stack

GoHighLevel (CRM/automation platform mentioned), AI agents for review generation and referral automation. Meta ads (Facebook/Instagram) for lead generation.

Client Acquisition

Inbound sales calls. Prospects book calls after seeing YouTube content or ads. Also teaches others to build AI automation agencies.

Sales Process

1. Introduction (break ice, where are you located, weather). 2. Discovery - diagnose problem with specific numbers (revenue, cost per acquisition, member count). 3. Build doubt by asking about current processes for reviews/referrals. 4. Identify goals and show current path wont reach them. 5. Transition to expert positioning. 6. Pitch tailored AI agent solution. 7. Temperature check (is she sold before price?). 8. Price pitch. 9. Collect payment, get agreements signed, outline next steps.

Objection Handling

When prospect is already working with another agency: find the gap in their service offering and sell the specific AI solution the competitor doesnt provide. When prospect says they have a review process: chunk down with numbers - she claimed to have a process but had zero reviews in 3 months, proving the system doesnt work.

Contrarian Insight

Even when a prospect works with a competitor agency, you can still close the deal by selling a specific AI solution the competitor doesnt offer. Low-ticket AI agent as foot-in-the-door leads to $16K+ in upsells. Dont accept I have a process at face value - always chunk down to actual numbers.

Mistake Warning

Going into the price pitch without first doing a temperature check. If the prospect isnt sold on the solution before hearing the price, they will almost certainly not buy. Also: not getting prospects to share actual data/numbers during discovery - without data you cant accurately assess their situation.

Scalability

Low-ticket AI agent as entry point → team upsells to $8K packages → revenue share on results. One client turned into $16K+ in under 6 months. Model scales through sales team handling upsells while you close initial deals.

Best Quotes

This customer turned into over a $16,000 customer in less than six months.
If somebody has a process to do the thing your AI solution solves, that doesnt mean its dead in the water. Chunk down what that process looks like and see if its generating results.
If they dont share their data, youre not going to be able to accurately assess their situation. Literally say what do you mean you dont have it, lets pull it up.
I sell a whole system of AI services but in certain situations when people work with other agencies I can wiggle my way around because they dont provide all the services I can.

Action Steps

  • Use reviews/referrals AI agent as a foot-in-the-door offer that no other agency provides
  • Always get prospect to share actual numbers during discovery - dont accept vague answers
  • Do a temperature check before revealing price to ensure prospect is sold on the solution first
  • Structure for upsell: low-ticket AI agent → full service package ($8K+) → ongoing revenue share
Cameron England
$100K/mo AI Agency (License and Scale)		 Lead Gen Cameron England breaks down how to build an AI agency to $100K/month from scratc High-end home remodelers (ideal niche example). Businesses w $2,500 setup fee for AI infrastructure $500 per qualified booked in-home consultation (performance model) Personally scaled agency to $250K/month. Stud Partner flywheel: identify suppliers in niche (cab

Full Description

Cameron England breaks down how to build an AI agency to $100K/month from scratch. Key framework: niche selection using leverage (high ticket, underserved, recession resistant), no-brainer offer creation using Hormozi's $100M Offers, first 3 clients via partner flywheel or supplier partnerships, then scaling through paid ads and AI infrastructure. Personally scaled to $250K/month, helped others to multi-6 and 7 figures. Sells License and Scale program.

Pain Point

Remodelers get 1 new client = $20-50K revenue. Only need 1-2 clients/month for massive ROI. Wrong niche selection is biggest mistake (MedSpa = low leverage at $500-1K per treatment).

Pricing Justification

Bring 1 client/month at $50K project value = 10x ROI on $5K/month agency fee. Only 1-2 clients needed for massive ROI vs MedSpa needing 10-20 clients per month.

Delivery Process

1. Pick high-leverage niche 2. Create no-brainer offer using ChatGPT + $100M Offers framework 3. Get first 3 clients via partner flywheel (suppliers in industry) 4. Offer free 30-day trials 5. Crack fulfillment (most important) 6. Run ads + qualify leads + book calls 7. Build AI follow-up infrastructure 8. Scale team and delegate

Tools Stack

Full Description

Zack Kirk presents 3-tier framework of 7 AI agents by business maturity. STARTER (foot-in-door): (1) Google Review Agent - triggers post-service to auto-collect reviews boosting rankings; (2) Off-Hours Receptionist - answers during closed hours instead of voicemail. BREADWINNER (scale revenue): (3) Full-Time Receptionist - upsell from off-hours, replaces 60K/year human, 24/7 multiple calls; (4) AI Quoting Agent - trained on pricing rules to auto-generate quotes without owner time; (5) AI Reactivation Agent - re-engages dead CRM leads via automated follow-up. PREMIUM: (6) Internal Ops Agents - scheduling, dispatching, inventory automation; (7) AI Workshop and Team Training - structured programs teaching teams AI in their roles.

Pain Point

Low review volume hurting Google ranking. Missed calls to voicemail = lost revenue. Human receptionist 60K/year, one call at a time, sick days. Hours daily of FREE unpaid labor giving quotes that get ghosted. Dead leads collecting dust in CRM. Employees threatened by AI.

Pricing Justification

Full-time: 60K human vs 10K + 1K/month (22K/year) = saves 38K/year AND better performance. Quoting removes hours of daily unpaid labor. Reactivation: warm leads just need follow-up. Training: 10x productivity.

Delivery Process

Review: low risk just more reviews. Off-Hours: captures leads instead of voicemail. Full-Time: 60K human vs 22K AI with multiple calls 24/7. Start with starters to build trust before premium.

Isaiah, from Liam Ottley's original 8, built an AI appointment setter business doing $25K/month entirely organically. Evolved from targeting coaches/consultants to serving influencers and small enterprises at scale. Non-technical founder who found a technical co-founder through community.

Pain Point

As businesses grow, they can't personally respond to all inbound DMs. Setter teams can't keep up with volume. High-priority leads (verified accounts, 50K+ followers) need human escalation. Need 24/7 automated qualification without losing personal touch.

Pricing Justification

Delivery Process

Integrate ManyChat + VoiceFlow + Airtable 5. Client receives ManyChat link - plug and play 6. Custom Slack channel for ongoing communication 7. Co-pilot model with human escalation thresholds

ManyChat (trigger and front-end), VoiceFlow (conversation intelligence), Airtable (database structure), Replet (early prototyping), Slack (client communication). Triggers: comments, story replies, ad replies, keyword intents.

Client Acquisition

Objection Handling

Coaches want personal touch: co-pilot model with human escalation for high-value leads. Cost concern: one deployment handles volume that would require multiple human setters. Trust: reverse-engineer their actual conversation style so AI sounds like them.

Contrarian Insight

Appointment setting is one of the hardest AI problems to solve - conversations and building trust are incredibly dynamic. The niche evolved from coaches/consultants to influencers/small enterprises because influencer audiences are more accepting of automation. You don't need to be technical - Isaiah is a non-developer who found a Stanford-trained co-founder through community. Product, process, people - in that order.

Mistake Warning

Thinking you need to be a developer to build AI systems. Giving up because the problem is hard - appointment setting took a year to get right. Not finding a technical co-founder. Not leveraging community for both learning and client acquisition. Chasing revenue before building solid product and processes.

Scalability

You can go to college and spend 100 grand and waste four years or you can do this for a year and you're going to do great

Full Description

Custom AI Studio architects, designs, builds, and deploys custom AI systems for mid-market companies and enterprises. They evolved from $2,500 automations to $400K-$500K annual projects with ongoing managed service contracts. The core thesis is repositioning from a lifestyle AI agency into a company with real enterprise value targeting a $100M exit. They provide AI readiness consulting, custom system development, and ongoing managed services, focusing on the early majority of companies now coming online to AI.

Pain Point

Companies know they need AI but do not know how to implement it. The early majority is being pressured by Claude/ChatGPT wave to actually do something. Internal teams lack expertise to build production AI systems despite tools getting easier.

Pricing Justification

Value of development trending toward zero means expertise/strategy is the real value. Companies need guides through AI transformation, not just builders. The 67-year-old lawyer can vibe-code decent apps now - pure development is commoditized.

Delivery Process

1. Initial AI readiness assessment and consulting. 2. Architecture and design of custom AI systems. 3. Build and develop the solutions. 4. Deploy into production. 5. Ongoing managed services and partnerships.

Tools Stack

Claude/Anthropic ecosystem (primary driver of current wave), OpenAI/GPT ecosystem, custom AI systems. Specific tools not detailed - focus is on strategy over tooling.

Client Acquisition

YouTube thought leadership (viral video 18 months of building autonomous agents), referrals from existing clients, inbound from content marketing.

Outreach Script

Not provided - inbound/thought-leadership driven

Sales Process

1. Thought leadership attracts inbound interest. 2. Discovery of client AI needs. 3. Scope project architecture. 4. Multi-hundred-thousand dollar proposal. 5. Year-long engagement with managed services.

Objection Handling

Not detailed in transcript

Contrarian Insight

The value of actually doing development is trending towards zero. Most people in the AI agency space are thinking lifestyle business when they should be thinking enterprise value and exit. A 67-year-old lawyer can vibe-code decent apps now - pure dev work is collapsing. Most people probably should not do their own thing.

Mistake Warning

Thinking of your AI agency as a lifestyle business instead of building real enterprise value. Continuing to sell development hours when the market for that is collapsing. Not escaping to where the puck is going.

Scalability

Small automations ($2,500) -> Mid-size projects -> $400K-$500K annual engagements with managed services -> Build to $6M+ revenue -> Sell at 5x for $30M+ -> Target $100M exit.

Best Quotes

The value of actually doing development is trending towards zero. It is like very very stark and very very clear that it is happening.
AI is not its own bucket. Every single vertical will just have AI seeping into it.
When I get a 67 year old lawyer coming to me with a vibe-coded app that is like decent, that is obviously signal that the talent and the skill set of doing development... that whole thing is collapsing.
As a pure AI readiness consultant, 2 million a year, right? So your profit margin, you are going to be able to sell that business for probably $2 million. But if you are making six million a year, then it is like, Oh, now I can sell this business for

Action Steps

  • Reframe your AI agency from lifestyle business to enterprise-value business
  • Stop selling development hours and start selling AI readiness consulting and strategic transformation
  • Build thought leadership content to attract mid-market inbound leads
  • Develop managed service contracts for recurring revenue and higher exit multiples
  • Target the early majority companies whose executives are now being pressured to adopt AI
TK Kader / Unstoppable
5 SaaS Agent Ideas You Can Build and Sell This Wee		 SaaS TK Kader (Unstoppable) shares 5 specific SaaS agent ideas targeting mid-market a Mid-market account executives and salespeople. Commission-ba Free trial / freemium start $30/month per user (can expand to $99+/month with more data integration) ToutApp: 100,000 registered users, 10,000 pay Product-led growth, bottoms-up. Free trial → immed

Full Description

TK Kader (Unstoppable) shares 5 specific SaaS agent ideas targeting mid-market account executives. His framework: mid-market AEs are the ideal ICP because they have money (salary + commission), wont vibe-code solutions themselves, and are incentivized to pay for tools that increase win rates. Each agent is $30/month, product-led growth, bottoms-up sale. Ideas follow deal lifecycle: 1) Similar ICP Agent (finds 5 lookalike companies from won deals), 2-5 not fully captured in first 12K chars. He built and sold ToutApp (backed by a16z, acquired by Marketo, then sold to Adobe for $4.75B).

Pain Point

Mid-market AEs handle high volume of deals with long ramp times. Churn-and-burn culture means most reps fail out. AEs are looking for an edge to beat peers. CRMs (Salesforce, HubSpot) are just database companies - product managers go home at 5pm. Huge opportunity to build 10x better tools.

Pricing Justification

AEs earn commission. If the tool helps them win more deals, they make more money and gladly pay $30/month for the edge. Its giving them a slice of their increased commission.

Delivery Process

1. Build SaaS agent targeting one of 5 deal lifecycle stages. 2. Free trial with immediate value demonstration. 3. $30/month subscription billing. 4. Engineer virality so other salespeople discover tool. 5. Land individual reps, expand to teams, upsell enterprise licenses.

Tools Stack

Chrome extension (for Gmail-based ideas), CRM integrations (Salesforce, HubSpot), AI agents for prospecting/deal intelligence. SaaS subscription billing. PLG onboarding.

Client Acquisition

Product-led growth, bottoms-up. Free trial → immediate value → credit card. Engineer virality within sales teams. No sales calls needed initially.

Sales Process

1. User discovers tool (viral spread within sales org). 2. Free trial with first 5 deals free. 3. See immediate value (similar ICP companies identified). 4. Slide credit card at $30/month. 5. Share with team → manager buys enterprise license.

Objection Handling

Why not just use ChatGPT/Gemini? People will pay for convenience AND expertise. Not every salesperson knows the exact prompts. The custom alpha is the specialized prompts and workflow integration inside their existing tools (Gmail, CRM).

Contrarian Insight

SaaS is not dead - AI is making SaaS stronger than ever. More than 50% of ChatGPT revenue is subscription revenue. The recurring revenue model is stronger than ever. Anytime theres copy-paste into ChatGPT, theres an opportunity for a SaaS product. Target people who go home at 5pm (big company PMs) because startups can always crush those products.

Mistake Warning

Pursuing multiple ideas simultaneously. The years TK focused on one thing, everything worked better. The worst place to be as a founder is neither here nor there. Pick one of these five ideas and go deep.

Scalability

5 ideas as separate companies, could eventually roll into one platform. Each follows subscription model with viral expansion within sales organizations. $30/month × thousands of reps across many companies.

Best Quotes

Salespeople are like founders and entrepreneurs, except they have more money than us. They get paid commission and salary.
Theyre like captains of football teams versus the nerd engineer. Theyre like look, Ill pay you money, give me the agent. If it makes more money, Ill keep paying you more money.
I like picking on companies where I know the product manager working on the product goes home at 5pm because thats what they signed up for.
SaaS is not dead and AI is making SaaS stronger than ever. More than 50% of ChatGPT revenues are actually subscription revenue.

Action Steps

  • Build a Similar ICP Agent as Chrome extension that analyzes won deals and finds 5 lookalike companies monthly
  • Price at $30/month with free trial, target mid-market AEs who earn commission and will pay for tools that increase win rates
  • Engineer virality into the product so it spreads within sales teams naturally
  • Start bottoms-up PLG - no sales calls needed, let the product sell itself through immediate value demonstration

0-Employee AI Content Agency		 Content Creation Barbara from Startup Cookie runs a 6-figure content agency with zero employees u Startups, SaaS founders, CEOs who want thought leadership co Not specified 6-figure annual revenue implies significant monthly retainers per client. Tool costs under $1K/year. 6-figure agency revenue with zero employees. Not detailed in transcript. Implied: referrals, co

Full Description

Barbara from Startup Cookie runs a 6-figure content agency with zero employees using AI. Started as head of content at YC startups, realized AI could replace the entire team she managed ($100K+/year in freelancers). Key insight: never start from blank prompt - always use transcripts from founder interviews, webinars, or podcasts as source material. Co-founder interviews founders monthly, then AI repurposes across all channels.

Pain Point

Managing content teams is expensive ($100K+/year in freelancers) and painful (60-70% of freelancer output needs rewriting). Founders can't commit time to content creation. Getting 5 writers, SEO copywriters, video editors, audio editors is prohibitively expensive.

Pricing Justification

Replaces $100K+/year content team. Average client stays 1.5 years. AI tools cost under $1K/year total. Quality comparable to $200-300/article freelancers with 60-70% less rewriting needed.

Delivery Process

1. Monthly 1-hour founder interview (co-founder conducts) 2. Transcribe using Granola 3. Load transcript + brand guidelines into Claude/ChatGPT project 4. AI generates blog posts from transcript 5. Repurpose to social media, email, other channels 6. Use existing webinar recordings as additional source 7. Build persona over time with 10-20+ transcripts 8. Team can query AI persona for founder's perspective on trending topics

Tools Stack

ChatGPT, Claude (projects with all context), Granola (meeting transcripts with chat), Riverside, Midjourney, Descript, Super Whisper, OpenAI. Total cost under $1K/year.

Client Acquisition

Not detailed in transcript. Implied: referrals, content marketing, startup ecosystem connections from YC background.

Sales Process

Not detailed in transcript

Objection Handling

AI quality concern: 'Not only is this better than the usual draft I get delivered, I can work with it, and I don't have to wait 2-3 weeks.' Key: never prompt from blank - always use real transcripts as source material.

Contrarian Insight

Never start AI content from a blank prompt. Always have a real transcript, interview, or webinar recording as source material. The first time it clicked was using webinar transcripts for blog posts - exponentially better quality. Most $200-300/article freelancers produce work that needs 60-70% rewriting anyway. Building an AI persona of the founder from 10-20 transcripts means team can query 'what would the founder say about X' without bothering them.

Mistake Warning

Going into ChatGPT and prompting 'write an article on X topic' from blank. Not having genuine source material (transcripts, interviews). Hiring full content teams when AI + one interview/month produces equivalent output. Trying to automate everything - use AI at every step but don't fully automate.

Scalability

Head of content skills -> Realize AI replaces team -> Start agency with first client -> Interview-based content system -> Build founder personas -> Scale to 6 figures with zero employees -> 1.5 year average retention

Best Quotes

Not only is this better than the usual draft I get delivered, I can work with it, and I don't have to wait two three weeks
We never start with just prompting AI by itself to do anything, even the simplest social media post. We always have something to start with
I was essentially managing an entire team of people just to sustain a pretty mid content schedule
It's kind of endless if you have that good starting piece

Action Steps

  • Identify your best source material: webinar recordings, podcast episodes, meeting transcripts
  • Set up a Claude project with brand guidelines and 3+ transcripts
  • Test: generate one blog post from a real transcript vs blank prompt and compare quality
  • Pitch 3 founders: monthly 1-hour interview in exchange for full content management
  • Calculate cost savings vs hiring freelance content team
Alex Hormozi
How to Win With AI in 2026		 Consulting Hormozi lays out a strategic framework for how businesses should restructure aro Main Street businesses of all sizes, entrepreneurs starting Not applicable - this is strategic framework, not a specific service Not applicable directly, but Hormozi notes that if something cost $2,000/month (with embedded labor cost), you can still charge $2,000 but your cost drops from $500 to $50 or $5/month - massive margin expansion Companies in his portfolio achieving millions Not a client acquisition video - it's strategic ed

Full Description

Hormozi lays out a strategic framework for how businesses should restructure around AI. Not a specific product to sell, but a philosophy: stop thinking in roles (I need an editor) and start thinking in workflows (what are the 5 things an editor does, and can each be automated?). He advocates BYOS/BYOA (Bring Your Own Software/Agents) where individuals become entire departments by training AI agents. His companies achieve millions per employee per year in revenue because they started AI-first from day one. He also identifies health, entertainment, and housing as industries that will survive the AI disruption.

Pain Point

Zero net job creation in the private sector (Jerome Powell data). Businesses organized around roles and headcount rather than workflows and outputs. Most people don't train AI the way they would train an employee - they try once, get bad output, and give up. Price sensitivity is slow to adapt to new cost structures, creating massive margin opportunity. 20 hours to become proficient but people delay the first hour for decades.

Pricing Justification

Revenue per employee in millions per year at Hormozi's AI-first companies. One person can now be an entire marketing department (cites Anthropic having one person in their marketing department). The coordination cost between humans is the biggest cost of scaling - AI eliminates it.

Delivery Process

ORGANIZATIONAL REDESIGN: 1. Write down EVERY task you do daily at the most granular level (not 'I run ads' but 'I make campaigns, set budgets, analyze results, make creative, write copy, test headlines'). 2. For every hire you're considering, list the 4-10 things that person actually does with their hands/eyes/mouth. 3. Ask whether each activity could live inside a workflow instead of headcount. 4. Take the first task and put it into AI: 'Help me automate this. What steps would you take?' 5. Take the first thing on that list and do it. 6. If stuck, screenshot your screen and ask 'What do I do now?' 7. Repeat the feedback loop - 100 cycles might take a year with a person but 100 minutes with AI. AI TRAINING: 1. Define 12 rules the AI can never break. 2. Provide 16+ writing samples. 3. Say 'write only according to this.' 4. Repeat feedback loop 100+ times. 5. Output improves exponentially compared to human training.

Tools Stack

AI agents (general - not platform-specific), automation workflows, whatever tools the business currently uses. Hormozi doesn't recommend specific tools - he recommends the framework of decomposing roles into tasks and automating each task.

Client Acquisition

Not a client acquisition video - it's strategic education. However, Hormozi notes the massive opportunity in going to regular businesses and automating portions of their work. The BYOS/BYOA model means approaching a business and saying 'I am your entire marketing department.'

Outreach Script

Not provided as scripts. But the positioning: 'I am your entire marketing department' - approach businesses as a one-person department replacement powered by AI agents trained on your methodology.

Sales Process

Not a sales process video. Strategic framework for organizational transformation.

Objection Handling

'AI can't do what humans can do' - Hormozi destroys this: Humans learn through reinforcement (do thing, get outcome, adjust). Computers are better at recognizing patterns than humans. The problem is most people don't define what good looks like - they expect AI to guess. If you define 12 rules and provide 16 writing samples, output is 5x better immediately. 'I'm too old for this' - All the people with all the money are over 50 and saying this. Their money will quickly go to people who adapted. 'What about AI safety?' - Like saying you got hacked so you should never use the internet again. Not good reasoning.

Contrarian Insight

Stop organizing businesses around roles and org charts - organize around linear workflows that create outputs. Org charts exist to organize communication between humans and hierarchy for decision-making, but if you had rules from the beginning, every task should be organized in a linear fashion creating an output. Also: if you're not automating your own job, someone else will automate it for you. Also: look at what porn adopts first - it eventually makes its way down to mainstream (AI avatars, chatbot conversations, zero filming/drama).

Mistake Warning

Trying once with AI, getting bad output, and concluding it will never work. You would not fire a brand new employee after one bad deliverable. Also: not being willing to have hard conversations about roles that have been automated away. If you don't, a startup that never had those roles will beat you. Also: thinking too short-term about the learning investment.

Scalability

One person becomes entire department via BYOS/BYOA. Revenue per employee in millions. Coordination cost between humans (biggest scaling bottleneck) drops to zero. Margin expansion from labor cost arbitrage. Phase shift coming - swimming in water that is about to boil into gas.

Best Quotes

AI will never be worse than it is right now.
It takes about 20 hours to become proficient in any new skill, but people delay the first hour decades.
Stop thinking in role-based thinking and start thinking in workflow-based thinking.
Revenue per employee we're talking about in the millions per year per head because day one we started that way.

Action Steps

  • Write down every task you do daily at the most granular level - not role titles but actual hand/eye/mouth activities
  • Take the first task and put it into AI: 'Help me automate this. What steps would you take?' - then do step 1
  • Block a full weekend (Saturday + Sunday) to sit in front of the computer and build one automated workflow end to end
  • For every hire you're considering, list the 4-10 actual tasks and evaluate each for workflow automation
  • Download free 10-stage roadmap at acquisition.com/roadmap for the zero-to-$100M+ progression
JP Middleton
Selling AI Systems Live (Gym Members Now)		 Lead Reactiv. JP Middleton's AI agency Gym Members Now (valued at $5.4M) sells AI systems to l Local service businesses: gyms, fitness studios, dental prac $1,500+/month AI system (from live call example) $1,500/month (demonstrated in live call) $6 million in profit in 4 years. Agency value Facebook group prospecting, outreach to local busi

Full Description

JP Middleton's AI agency Gym Members Now (valued at $5.4M) sells AI systems to local businesses that generate reviews, referrals, and reactivate old databases. The system follows up with customers after service to get reviews, then asks reviewers for referrals with incentives, and AI calls/texts old leads to reactivate them. Live sales call recording shows exact discovery and pitch process.

Pain Point

Businesses do terrible job asking for reviews. Staff doesn't follow up. No system to generate referrals from reviews. Old leads sitting in database untouched. No CRM or automated follow-up. Spending on ads without maximizing existing customer base.

Pricing Justification

If client only asks for reviews after prompting (not systematically), they're losing referrals. If old database of leads is untouched, that's free revenue sitting idle. Historical revenue analysis often shows decline - system reverses that.

Delivery Process

1. Discovery call with pillar-based questions 2. Diagnose problems with specific numbers 3. Build doubt around current pillars 4. Clarify goals and timeline 5. Present AI system as solution 6. Deploy review generation AI 7. Deploy referral system with incentives 8. Deploy database reactivation 9. Deploy lead follow-up automation

Tools Stack

AI review generation system, AI referral system with incentive tracking, database reactivation via AI calling/texting, CRM integration, automated follow-up sequences

Client Acquisition

Facebook group prospecting, outreach to local business owners, consultative selling approach

Outreach Script

Not a cold script - discovery-based sales. Key question: 'Do you ask for referrals after someone leaves you a review?' (Answer is always no). Review incentive: 'If you give us your feedback, you'll have a chance to win a membership for free.'

Sales Process

1. Introduction and rapport building 2. Discovery: diagnose problem with specific numbers 3. Build doubt around current approach (pillar-based questions) 4. Clarify personal and business goals 5. Historical revenue analysis 6. Present system as solution to diagnosed problems 7. Close on monthly subscription

Objection Handling

Client doing well already: expose what they're NOT doing (reviews, referrals, reactivation). Client worked with another agency: 'Did they help with reviews? Referrals? Database? Why not?' - exposes gaps. No time: AI handles it all automatically.

Contrarian Insight

Don't start with ads if low-hanging fruit exists in reviews, referrals, and old database. Most agencies sell lead gen but ignore the goldmine already sitting in the business. The question 'Do you ask for referrals after someone leaves a review?' has a 100% 'no' answer rate and instantly creates desire.

Mistake Warning

Jumping straight to running ads when the business hasn't maximized existing customer base. Not asking pillar-based questions in discovery. Not getting specific numbers (leads, appointments, shows, closes, revenue, expenses). Not doing historical revenue analysis.

Scalability

Single market agency -> Multiple clients in niche -> Consult others to replicate model -> Roll up businesses into portfolio -> Nine-figure exit

Best Quotes

Do you ask for referrals after someone leaves you a review? Because the answer is always no
If you give us your feedback, you'll have a chance to win a membership for free for the year
Why would you go into running ads if you hadn't done anything with these things over here?
You want to chunk down their problem with specific numbers

Action Steps

  • Create a pillar-based discovery questionnaire for your niche
  • Build a review generation and referral system with AI follow-up
  • Practice the historical revenue analysis question on calls
  • Identify 10 local businesses with reviews but no referral system
  • Build demo of AI review-to-referral pipeline

Full Description

Comprehensive 3-part course covering: (1) AI agent foundations - what agents are, 5 components (brain/LLM, prompt, memory, knowledge, tools), APIs, how tools work; (2) Four end-to-end AI agent build tutorials including voice AI receptionist, chatbot with custom tools, sales rep co-pilot with N8N + Lovable frontend + Airtable CRM + LinkedIn scraping + Firecrawl web scraping; (3) Monetization via two paths - Builder (technical, hands-on building for clients) or Consultant (audits, strategy sessions, team training, passing builds to builders for a cut). Built AI agents for NBA teams, Fortune 500 companies, and publicly traded companies.

Pain Point

95% of AI projects fail not because the technology doesn't work but because businesses don't have people to implement it properly (MIT data). Companies rushing into AI, buying tools, starting projects, watching them fall apart. 57% of all work Americans do today can be automated with existing technology (McKinsey 2025). 92 million jobs displaced by 2030 (World Economic Forum). Workers with AI skills earn 56% more and that gap doubled in 1 year.

Pricing Justification

56% wage premium for AI skills that doubled in 1 year. 66% of employers actively trying to hire people who understand AI. Only 12% of workers have taken any AI training - massive supply gap. 1:1,100 ratio of AI providers to businesses needing help.

Delivery Process

AGENT BUILDING FRAMEWORK (3 ingredients): 1. Prompting - write clear instructions in plain language for agent behavior. 2. Knowledge - give agent company-specific info (PDFs, spreadsheets, transcripts). 3. Tools - connect APIs so agent can take actions (calendar, email, CRM, web search). BUILD EXAMPLES FROM VIDEO: Voice AI receptionist, Chatbot with knowledge base and custom tools, Sales rep co-pilot with N8N backend + Lovable frontend + Airtable CRM + SerpAPI search + Apify LinkedIn scraping + Firecrawl web scraping + Gmail integration. SALES REP CO-PILOT: Agent pulls call transcript from CRM, searches Google for prospect info, scrapes LinkedIn profile, scrapes company website, writes pre-call briefing, sends emails, updates CRM notes - all through conversational interface.

Tools Stack

N8N (workflow automation backend - free self-hosted), Lovable (AI-powered frontend/app builder), Airtable (CRM/database - free tier), SerpAPI (Google search API for agent research), Apify ($29/month paid plan for LinkedIn scraper actor), Firecrawl (web scraping API - free tier), Gmail API (email sending), OpenAI/Anthropic LLMs (agent brain), Vapi or similar (voice AI), Webhook triggers (connecting frontend to backend)

Client Acquisition

BUILDER: 1. Join communities (Liam's free School community with 280,000+ people). 2. Build reputation as reliable and technically solid. 3. Get client opportunities from referrals within community. 4. Upwork for additional deal flow. CONSULTANT: 1. Attend in-person trade shows and industry events. 2. Talk to business owners about AI. 3. Conversations turn into paid discovery sessions. 4. Discovery sessions turn into larger projects passed to builders.

Outreach Script

Not provided as verbatim scripts. Builder path relies on reputation and community referrals. Consultant path relies on in-person events and warm outreach.

Sales Process

BUILDER: 1. Join community. 2. Help people, build reputation. 3. Get referral. 4. Scope and build project. 5. Deliver and get more referrals. CONSULTANT: 1. Attend events. 2. Have AI conversations with business owners. 3. Offer free AI audit. 4. Progress to paid audit. 5. Identify build opportunities. 6. Pass to builder, take a cut.

Objection Handling

Kevin O'Leary: 'Anything to do with AI. In the simplest form, it's helping people use the technology. There's going to be a massive amount of people wanting to use it that don't know how.' Mark Cuban: 'The biggest opportunity right now is helping small to medium-sized businesses who don't understand AI yet but desperately need it.' Naval Ravikant: 'Go brush up on your AI, learn a little bit, watch a few videos, use the AI, tinker with it, and then go reapply for that job that rejected you, and watch how they pull you in.'

Contrarian Insight

You don't need to build the next ChatGPT to make money with AI. The real opportunity is much simpler - helping businesses understand and use AI. 95% of AI projects fail not because the tech doesn't work but because businesses lack implementers. The consultant path makes money without building anything - audit, strategize, train, then pass builds to others for a cut. Also: the 56% AI wage premium that doubled in 1 year is the early mover advantage - it won't last forever.

Mistake Warning

Trying to be both builder AND consultant instead of picking one path. Giving up halfway through learning. Not picking a path and taking action - the 1:1,100 ratio won't last. Also technical: tool descriptions must be extremely detailed for AI agents to use them correctly - vague descriptions = broken tool calls.

Scalability

BUILDER: 2-3 clients/month > quit job > increase rates as reputation grows > hire other builders > agency model. CONSULTANT: free audits > paid audits > strategy sessions > team training > pass builds to builders for cut > build a consulting practice. Both paths: use community for deal flow, 30-day challenge to first paid client.

Best Quotes

I've built multiple AI businesses that have generated over $10 million in revenue.
57% of all the work that Americans do today can be automated with the technology that already exists.
Workers who have AI skills are earning 56% more than people who don't, and that gap in pay has doubled in just 1 year.
Only 12% of workers have taken any AI training at all. That's not just a little gap, that's a canyon.

Action Steps

  • Start the 30-day challenge to get your first paid client using the free course materials in the community
  • Build the 4 agent types from the tutorial (voice receptionist, chatbot, custom tools agent, sales rep co-pilot) as portfolio pieces
  • If Builder: set up profiles on Upwork and start building reputation with small AI agent projects
  • If Consultant: identify 3 local trade shows or industry events in the next 30 days and attend to start conversations with business owners about AI

  • Read Empire of AI for full context on industry dynamics
  • Consider the ethical implications when positioning AI services to businesses
  • Build AI services that augment rather than wholesale replace workers
  • Be transparent with clients about AI limitations vs marketing hype
  • Diversify your business model so you're not dependent on one AI company's platform

N/A

Contrarian Insight

Build a real business with a product, not a cool demo. If you can't explain your product value in 10 words, you're not close to a real business. Don't automate quality out of existence - use AI at every step but maintain human curation. Validate by manually creating and emailing results to prospects before building any product. Mistake Warning

We manually created the results and just emailed them to all potential customers and got more than 60% positive feedback

Action Steps

  • Validate your AI product idea by manually delivering results to 20 prospects
  • Measure: do 60%+ give positive feedback and ask for more?
  • Build MVP as Discord bot or simple interface, not full product
  • Track retention and engagement, not just 'amazing' feedback
  • Watch for complaints about queue times and usage limits as PMF signals

Full Description

Argues that selling AI automations (N8N tools, small automations) is one of the worst business models in 2026 because its cheap ($50-200), commoditized (Fiverr/Upwork competition), and AI will automate the automation builders. Instead, offers a model selling simple AI systems to local businesses: lead generation through Meta AI ads + AI voice callers to book appointments. Uses ArcAds for AI video ads ($7/video). Charges $10K-$25K upfront for 3 months plus upsells and revenue share. Works exclusively with stem cell clinics and peptide clinics as niche.

Pain Point

Local businesses see AI on the news daily but have no clue how to implement it. They need leads and appointments but cant generate them effectively. The gap between AI demand and implementation ability is where the money is.

Pricing Justification

AI ads crush right now on Meta. Lead gen + AI calling = measurable ROI. Stem cell/peptide clients are high-ticket medical treatments with high customer lifetime value.

Delivery Process

1. Choose one niche (stick with it 3-6 months). 2. Generate AI video ads using ArcAds ($7/video) or Ideogram (image ads). 3. Run Meta ads for client lead generation. 4. Use AI callers (Retail AI) to call and book leads. 5. Track in GoHighLevel CRM. 6. Use paid ads for own client acquisition.

Tools Stack

ArcAds.ai (AI video ads, $7/video, lip-synced actors), Ideogram (AI image ads), GoHighLevel (CRM), Retail AI (voice AI callers), Meta ads platform (Facebook/Instagram), Claude/ChatGPT (ad scripts). Andromeda Meta update mentioned for AI creative optimization.

Client Acquisition

Paid ads exclusively. Cold outreach, email blasts, SMS blasts, cold calling described as what YouTube gurus lie about. Every big agency owner uses paid ads for predictable inbound.

Outreach Script

Uses AI-generated video ad scripts. Example shown: Are you a real estate agent still struggling to get more listings in 2025? In the next 60 seconds Im going to show you how my team and I are helping over 100 agents like yourself make more money...

Sales Process

1. Run paid ads targeting your chosen niche. 2. Prospects book inbound sales calls. 3. Close on $10K-$25K upfront + 3-month commitment. 4. Deliver lead gen + AI calling. 5. Upsell additional services and revenue share.

Objection Handling

AI ads look fake: most people on social media have brains turned off and dont notice. Even if they do, engagement still happens. Addresses AI will take over automation building: thats exactly why selling automations is bad - the skill is prompt engineering not building.

Contrarian Insight

Selling AI automations is one of the WORST business models in 2026. Its low ticket ($50-200), anyone on Fiverr can do it cheaper, and AI tools like Claude and GoHighLevel are automating the automation builders themselves. The real model is being a strategic partner who solves lead gen + appointment booking problems, not a technician who builds N8N workflows.

Mistake Warning

Starting an AI automation agency selling cheap $50-200 builds. Low-ticket clients are the worst type of clients. Fiverr/Upwork competition will undercut you. AI itself will replace automation builders. Instead, charge $10K-$25K for strategic lead gen + AI systems.

Scalability

Master one niche → duplicate delivery across clients in same vertical. AI ads cost $7/video to produce. Paid ads for own acquisition create predictable inbound pipeline. Scale ad spend as results prove out.

Best Quotes

Building and selling AI automations is one of the worst business models you can start in 2026 with the success rate being at an all-time low.
A lot of the times its $50, $100, $200 to build some N8N tool. Businesses can find someone on Fiverr to do this for a fraction of the cost.
I charge 10, 15, $25,000 upfront for my service. And thats just for 3 months. That doesnt even include the upsells or the revenue share.
Gone are the days of having to be technical. The skill is prompt engineering.

Action Steps

  • Stop selling cheap AI automations - charge $10K+ for strategic lead gen + AI systems
  • Use ArcAds for AI video ads at $7/video and run Meta ads for clients
  • Focus on one high-value niche (medical, home services) and stick with it 3-6 months
  • Run paid ads for your own client acquisition instead of cold outreach

Bundle all four pillars together because businesses reject individual tools but accept complete solutions. Mistake Warning

Selling AI tools individually (Hey do you want a chatbot? Hey do you want voice AI?). Got rejected for months. The breakthrough was bundling all four pillars and selling outcomes not technology. You dont sell voice. You dont sell chatbots. You sell the outcome.

Sells before building - validates demand with email outreach before creating the actual product. Uses Reddit for problem discovery, Gummy Search for subreddit scraping, AI for personalization at scale.

Pain Point

For PPC agencies: (1) Constantly asked to do SEO work they cant deliver, (2) Brand sniping/competitor strategies are effective but manual, (3) Finding PPC clients using PPC ads is competitive and expensive. For coaches: need client acquisition systems and scaling beyond their own time.

Pricing Justification

Craft timebound guarantees. 6. Scrape leads: Apollo, LinkedIn Jobs + Apify, AnyMailFinder. 7. Write personalized cold email copy with AI. 8. Create 16+ variants (2x2x2x2). 9. Send 1-3K emails/week via Instantly or SmartLead. 10. Track, drop losers, iterate winners. 11. Build only after buying interest.

Tools Stack

Gummy Search: Reddit audience research, finds subreddits by keyword, I hate searches, free/low cost. Apollo.io: B2B lead lists by industry/size/revenue. LinkedIn Jobs: Alternative lead source for hiring companies. Apify: Scrapes LinkedIn jobs, 1.20 per 1,000 leads. AnyMailFinder: Email finder, ~30% verified hit rate. Instantly/SmartLead: Cold email sending at scale. AI/LLM: Personalizes copy using scraped variables.

Client Acquisition

Cold email using 4-variable matrix. Scrape Apollo/LinkedIn, find emails via AnyMailFinder, send mass personalized emails. Key: test 16+ variants simultaneously instead of guessing.

Outreach Script

Hi, noticed you were hiring for [role]. I do [really cool thing] that solves [the need] using systems. I just delivered [amazing result] for somebody else. I believe so strongly I will guarantee [X] in 60 days or youre not paying a cent. Is this worth a chat?

Sales Process

1. Send 16+ variants. 2. Track reply rates. 3. Drop below-average. 4. Double down on winners. 5. Iterate copy. 6. From 78 replies: 34 opportunities, 27 positive, 19 meetings. 7. Sell idea before building. 8. Build only after confirmed intent.

Objection Handling

Timebound guarantees neutralize risk. Sell before build eliminates does this work objection by collecting real market data first.

Contrarian Insight

SELL BEFORE YOU BUILD. Dont spend more than 20 minutes picking a niche. Test 16+ combinations and let market data decide. There is no better tutor or mentor than the actual market.

Mistake Warning

Picking ONE niche, ONE offer, ONE scraping method, ONE copy variant. Chance of first-try success is very low. Test 16+ combinations. Also: dont target low-ticket (60/month = need 86 customers vs 1 at 5K).

Scalability

0-1: Run 3-hour sprint. 1-5: Drop losers, iterate winners to 5%+ reply rate. 5-20: Systematize, hire VAs, move to fractional COO model (3-15K/month). 20+: Productize the cold email infrastructure as a service.

Best Quotes

There is no better tutor or mentor than the actual market.
Rather than just trust me or somebody else because all this is speculation, why dont you just figure it out for yourself?
19 people in just a few hours with zero distribution asked us to have a meeting.
Its better to sell the thing before you build the thing.

Action Steps

  • Set up Gummy Search, search I hate in target subreddits by upvotes - find 3 problems in 30 minutes
  • Create 2x2x2x2 variant matrix = 16 testable campaigns
  • Apollo + AnyMailFinder to build 1,000+ decision-maker email list in high-ticket digital businesses
  • Write cold email with timebound guarantee and send 1,000+ via Instantly or SmartLead
  • Track reply rates per variant after 48 hours - kill below average, iterate winners

Full Description

Viral Reddit post (70K views) exposing the reality of AI automation agencies. The creator, an experienced AI developer who has worked both corporate and freelance, argues that most YouTube AI agency creators make their money from YouTube/courses/School communities, NOT from actually selling AI automations to businesses. Key findings: small businesses are not tech-savvy enough for sophisticated AI stacks, trust and readiness issues are massive barriers, GDPR/EU AI Act compliance creates huge obstacles in Europe, customer support automation is not the massive sell everyone claims because virtual assistants cost similar, and realistic solo agency revenue caps at ~$40K/month with 4-5 small-ticket clients.

Pain Point

The gap between what YouTube gurus promise ($100K/month AI agency) and reality. Small businesses dont trust AI systems, find integration difficult, and the actual revenue from selling automations is far lower than claimed. People in the gold rush are the ones selling shovels.

Pricing Justification

Business owners compare AI chatbot costs to virtual assistant costs and find them similar - until AI costs drop dramatically, customer support chatbots are not the massive sell everyone claims.

Delivery Process

What actually works: 1. AI consultation (tell people how to use AI in their business). 2. Implement specific automation for a very specific problem. 3. Focus on marketing/lead gen automations over customer support chatbots. 4. Include human-in-the-loop approach. 5. Build trust by being transparent about AI limitations.

Tools Stack

N8N (automation), Make.com, Vapi (voice AI), Gmail/Google Cloud/Calendar/Drive integrations. Warns about US-based tools (Make.com, Vapi) creating GDPR compliance issues for European clients since data cannot be sent to US.

Client Acquisition

Creator found it very challenging to sell to small businesses. Warns that cold outreach to local businesses without testimonials or reputation is extremely difficult.

Outreach Script

N/A - critiques the typical outreach model

Sales Process

N/A - this is a reality-check video

Objection Handling

Addresses the but they said $100K/month objection: 99% of Reddit commenters agreed that most AI influencers make money from YouTube/courses/communities, not from selling automations. Liam (considered founder of AAA model) was upfront that most of his money comes from AI education, not client work.

Contrarian Insight

People who make money in a gold rush are the ones selling shovels. The real money in AI automations is in the YouTube/School community/course business, not in actually selling AI automations to clients. Customer support automation is NOT the massive sell everyone claims - business owners can hire VAs for similar cost. Small businesses dont trust AI and the readiness is not there. Also: nobody talks about contracts, data agreements, GDPR, EU AI Act compliance.

Mistake Warning

Believing the $100K/month AI agency dream without understanding: 1) Small businesses are not tech-savvy enough for sophisticated AI stacks. 2) Trust/readiness barriers are massive. 3) GDPR and data protection laws create real obstacles. 4) Customer support chatbots cost similar to virtual assistants. 5) Nobody talks about contracts and data agreements. 6) AI agents are still unpredictable - one stupid output destroys client trust.

Scalability

Realistically caps at ~$40K/month solo with 4-5 clients before needing to hire and scale linearly. Education/community model (School at $80/seat/month) is more scalable and profitable than client service work.

Best Quotes

People who make money in a gold rush are the ones who are selling the shovels.
99% of the people agreed that most of these content creators actually are not making their money through AI.
Small businesses are not so tech-savvy. Its really hard to integrate such a high sophisticated tech stack into their original tech stack.
The next time you see someone posting about 100k a month, just know that it is possible they making this money but not through AI automations.

Action Steps

  • Focus on AI consultation + specific problem implementation rather than generic chatbot selling
  • Target marketing/lead gen automations over customer support chatbots - higher perceived value
  • Always include human-in-the-loop to build trust and handle AI failures gracefully
  • Be realistic about revenue expectations: solo agency caps around $40K/month with 4-5 clients

Contrarian Insight

Most people skip spam prevention and dont understand the relationship between concurrency and CPS. Default CPS of 1 means one call initiated per second. If you have 20 concurrent calls with average 5-minute duration, you need to calculate whether your CPS can fill the concurrency pool efficiently. Also: out of the box, most phone numbers WILL be marked as spam - you must proactively verify and brand them.

Mistake Warning

Not understanding the concurrency vs CPS relationship leads to overspending on concurrency or getting numbers blocked. Also: if you have other agents running with incoming calls, batch calling concurrency allocation will steal from their available slots. Must balance concurrency across all agents.

Scalability

Scale outbound calling by: increasing concurrency slots ($8/each), using Telnyx for large-scale campaigns, triggering batch calls via API for automated workflows, managing multiple campaigns across niches.

Best Quotes

Out of the box most of these numbers will be marked as spam.
Telnyx numbers will be better for large scale outbound campaigns but if youre just starting out Twilio is fine.
Concurrency is the number of calls happening at the same time. CPS is calls per second - the flow into the pool.
If you have other agents running with calls coming in, you want to make sure concurrency isnt going to affect them.

Action Steps

  • Verify phone numbers and set up branded calling in Retail AI before running any outbound campaigns
  • Test phone numbers with Nomorobo and IP Quality Score to check spam status
  • Calculate concurrency needs: total leads / concurrency × average call duration = campaign time
  • Use Telnyx over Twilio for large-scale outbound campaigns
  • Balance concurrency allocation between outbound batch calls and inbound agent calls

The system calls previous clients who have been inactive for about a year, asks if they need repeat or additional services, and collects a yes/no response. Uses Retail AI for the voice agent, Make.com for the trigger automation, and Google Sheets as CRM representation. Specifically built for a roofing company example (roof cleaning, gutter cleaning) but adaptable to any service business. Only calls leads who have previously consented to marketing.

Pain Point

Business owners dont have the time or resources to call all old leads, so they never do it. These dead leads represent revenue sitting untapped in their CRM. AI voice agents now make it possible to systematically reactivate these leads at scale.

Pricing Justification

Reactivating even a small percentage of dormant leads generates immediate revenue for businesses that were leaving money on the table

Delivery Process

1. Set up Retail AI account and create single prompt voice agent. 2. Select AI model (GPT 5.1 recommended over 4.1). 3. Choose voice model (11 Labs default, multilingual option available). 4. Use ChatGPT to draft initial prompt using 4-part framework (Role, Task, Examples, Notes). 5. Paste prompt into Retail AI. 6. Build Make.com automation to trigger calls from Google Sheets/CRM lead list. 7. Test and iterate - modify Notes section based on call performance.

Tools Stack

Retail AI (voice agent platform - primary), Make.com (automation trigger workflow), Google Sheets (CRM representation/lead list management), ChatGPT (for drafting initial voice agent prompts), 11 Labs (voice synthesis within Retail AI), OpenAI GPT 5.1 or Claude models (LLM powering the agent)

Client Acquisition

YouTube content + free School community + premium AI Launchpad community launch

Outreach Script

Voice agent script framework: Role (who the agent is, personality, objective) → Task (specific objective like collect yes/no on service interest) → Examples (conversational examples of expected dialogue, FAQ Q&As) → Notes (iterative fixes based on testing)

Sales Process

Not specified for selling to clients

Objection Handling

Not specified

Contrarian Insight

Cold calling with AI is NOT allowed - you can only call leads who have previously consented to receive marketing materials. This is a legal compliance point most AI agency creators ignore. Lead reactivation specifically targets consented past clients, making it legally safe and higher-converting since they already know the business.

Mistake Warning

Do not skip the Examples section of your voice agent prompt - this is the most important part. Without good conversational examples, the voice agent goes off the rails and loses track. The Notes section should be used iteratively after testing calls to fix specific behaviors.

Scalability

System can be replicated across any service business vertical. Once the automation is built, it runs through entire lead lists automatically. Can be offered as a service to multiple clients with minimal customization (swap business details and services in the prompt).

Best Quotes

Business owners weve found dont have the time or resources to call all of these old leads, and they never end up doing it.
We are not able to send these AI generated calls to individuals that havent necessarily consented to receive any sort of marketing related information from the business. Cold calling isnt necessarily allowed.
Examples is actually one of the most important parts of your prompt. If youre unable to give it good examples, this is typically where the voice agent goes off the rails.
Notes are typically what I modify at the end of my tests. I call the agent and look at what is working well and what is not.

Action Steps

  • Use the 4-part prompt framework (Role, Task, Examples, Notes) as a template for any voice agent build
  • Always draft voice agent prompts in ChatGPT first before pasting into the voice platform - faster iteration
  • Only reactivate leads who have previously consented to marketing - never cold call with AI agents
  • Test the agent yourself first, document failures, then fix them in the Notes section of the prompt iteratively
