Full Description

Dan Wardrope demonstrates live a sales call selling his AI SMS reactivation system called the 'Sleeping Beauty Android' to a lead gen business owner. The system takes dead/unconverted leads and sends AI-powered SMS conversations to reactivate them and book appointments. 30% response rate on first SMS, roughly 10% of responders book appointments. Performance-based pricing with small deposit.

Pain Point

150 out of 200 daily leads are being lost - never contacted again. No CRM, no email follow-up, no SMS system. Sales team cant reach leads fast enough. People don't answer cold calls but respond to SMS.

Pricing Justification

100% upside for client: 150 leads/day sitting untouched are free money. 30% response rate on SMS vs near-zero on follow-up calls. 3 extra appointments per day from leads they were already losing. Skin in the game model aligns incentives.

Delivery Process

1. Get client's dead lead database 2. Build AI SMS agent trained on client's website/offer 3. Configure qualifying questions 4. Send initial 'prince charming' SMS to get response 5. AI qualifies and books appointments via SMS conversation 6. Monitor and iterate on copy/questions 7. Client provides sales script for AI refinement 8. Track attribution and revenue share

Tools Stack

AI SMS platform (custom built), client website scraping for AI training, SMS sending infrastructure, appointment booking integration, CRM for tracking

Client Acquisition

Facebook groups - finding business owners looking for leads. Warm outreach based on identified pain points. Book 'The Instant AI Agency' for framework.

Outreach Script

First SMS (Prince Charming): 'Hey, it's Sarah here from [business]. Is this the same [name] that was interested in [service]?' - Gets response first, doesn't force offer. Follow-up qualifies with 2-3 questions then books appointment.

Sales Process

1. Find prospect in Facebook group 2. Identify pain point (losing leads) 3. Share screen demo of AI SMS system 4. Let prospect interact with demo live 5. Show 30% response rate stats 6. Propose performance-based deal with small deposit 7. Close with zero-risk positioning

Objection Handling

Typing speed concern: add delays to simulate human typing. Formality: make messages shorter and more casual. How do you charge: performance-based, deposit refundable. Will people actually chat back: 30% response rate proven across hundreds of thousands of messages.

Contrarian Insight

The first SMS should NOT pitch the offer. Just get them to say 'yes' to confirm identity. People in 2025 prefer SMS over phone calls. Don't qualify heavily upfront if response rates drop - get them to appointment faster. Copywriting skills + AI = unstoppable combination for lead reactivation. The deposit is just skin-in-the-game, fully refundable.

Mistake Warning

Making AI messages too long and formal. Not adding typing delays (dead giveaway it's AI). Qualifying too aggressively before booking. Not getting client's actual sales script to train the AI. Starting without a deposit (client needs skin in the game).

Scalability

Demo to one client -> Prove 30% response rate -> Performance deal -> Replicate for any industry with dead leads -> Blue ocean opportunity across all businesses with databases

Best Quotes

30% of people that we send this message to respond. 30%
Out of 150 leads you're not talking to each day, probably 40 will respond to this message
It's performance based. We'd want some skin in the game from you
If we didn't get any results, we'd refund the money

Action Steps

  • Build an AI SMS reactivation demo for one industry
  • Write a 'prince charming' first message that gets identity confirmation
  • Test with 100 dead leads to validate 30% response rate
  • Create a performance-based pricing proposal template
  • Find 5 businesses in Facebook groups with large unconverted lead databases

SDR team can't reach leads fast enough. Leads go cold while waiting for human follow-up. Multiple product offerings require complex segmentation. Humans sleep but leads come in 24/7.

Pricing Justification

196 leads -> 164 activated -> 32 booked calls in one week. Each call worth 675 GBP (20% close rate x $4-20K products). ROI is immediate and measurable.

Delivery Process

1. Set up Appointwise agent (website scan auto-generates prompt) 2. Configure tonality, message delay, qualification priority 3. Set qualifying questions (1-3 max for simple, more for complex) 4. Configure follow-ups (5-10, including 30-day nurture) 5. Connect calendar (Google, GoHighLevel, Zapier) 6. Set AI tasks (tag unqualified, escalate high-value) 7. Import leads from CRM 8. AI sends outbound messages and handles inbound 9. Monitor via testing window

Tools Stack

Appointwise (AI sales agent platform - SMS, WhatsApp, Instagram, Facebook), GoHighLevel (CRM), Google Calendar, Zapier (integration), Clay.com (lead gen - Jordan is investor), School.com (community), Triple Whale (attribution)

Client Acquisition

Lead magnets (cold calling scripts), paid ads, YouTube content, Instagram DMs. AI agent handles all follow-up.

Outreach Script

AI outbound: 'Hey, it's [name] from [company]. Can you confirm this is [first_name]? Just checking, are you still interested in a free quote for solar? What made you curious about solar - bills, environment, something else? Do you own your property or rent?' Inbound lead magnet: qualifies before sending resource, then segments to appropriate product/calendar.

Sales Process

1. Lead enters through ad/lead magnet/DM 2. AI activates and sends first message 3. AI qualifies with 1-3 questions 4. AI handles objections conversationally 5. AI books call on appropriate calendar 6. Follow-ups continue for non-responders (5-10 messages over 30 days) 7. AI tags and segments in CRM based on responses

Objection Handling

Example from live conversation: Lead says 'I'm pretty happy where I'm at.' AI: 'I get that. You've achieved a lot already, but there's always room for growth, right? Would you be up for a quick call to explore how we can help with a game plan for launching those paid ads?' Lead pushes back on timing: AI offers alternative time. Pressure test: 'Can you cut to the chase?' AI: 'Understood. Let me get you booked.'

Contrarian Insight

Have 1-3 qualifying questions max for simple outbound. Follow up for 30 days, not just 5. Keep qualification priority on 'conservative' - don't push too hard for answers. Let the AI objection-handle - it's better than most humans at staying calm and persistent. 84% activation rate proves AI follow-up drastically outperforms human SDR teams.

Mistake Warning

Too many qualifying questions (causes drop-off). Not enough follow-ups (stop at 5 when 10+ over 30 days works better). Setting qualification priority too aggressive. Not testing the agent before deploying. Forgetting to set up CRM tags for different outcomes.

Scalability

Build for own business -> Prove results (32 calls/week) -> Sell as $2K+ service -> Scale across client base -> Platform investment/partnership

Best Quotes

32 sales calls booked in one week completely automatically without my intervention
This is a service we sell for at least $2,000 in my agency
Each of those sales calls is worth to us 675 pounds because our close rate is roughly 20%
I get that. You've achieved a lot already, but there's always room for growth, right?

Action Steps

  • Set up an AI sales agent for your own lead follow-up first
  • Import your unconverted leads and test activation rate
  • Configure 1-3 qualifying questions and 10 follow-up messages over 30 days
  • Track: activation rate, qualification rate, booked call rate
  • Once proven, package as $2K+ service for clients

Mistake Warning

Scalability

AI replaces most team members. One content manager can handle entire client roster using Fusion AI. Profit margins go from terrible (large team) to near-100% (AI + 1 person). Can take on unlimited clients without proportional hiring.

Action Steps

  • Use AI tools for content fulfillment (scripting, editing, ideation) to dramatically reduce team size and increase margins

Full Description

Two specific AI agents generating $85K/month. Agent 1: Outbound Speed-to-Lead Agent - connects to CRM, calls new form submissions within 20 seconds, qualifies leads, books appointments. Replaces employees waiting to dial leads. Agent 2: Internal Quoting Agent - chatbot connecting to part suppliers, applying labor rates/markup/fees/gas costs, generating accurate quotes in seconds instead of 10-15 minutes manually. Live demo shown for mobile mechanic: employee inputs vehicle model, describes issue, gets instant quote with parts, markup, labor, fees.

Pain Point

Speed-to-Lead: businesses lose customers because employees take 20-40 minutes to call back. If not contacted within 5 minutes, lead calls next business. Quoting: owners spend 2-3 hours daily on free quotes (20-50/day at 10-15 min each).

Pricing Justification

Speed-to-Lead: employee costs 5% commission ($4-5K/month) + bad speed-to-lead + training time. Agent costs $2-3K, calls in 20 seconds, scales. Quoting: saves 2-3 hours daily of owner highest-leverage time. 3 hours/day reduced to 5 minutes/day.

Delivery Process

Speed-to-Lead: 1. Connect to client CRM. 2. Set trigger for new form submissions. 3. Agent calls within 20 seconds. 4. Qualifies based on knowledge base. 5. Books appointment. Quoting: 1. Connect to parts supplier API. 2. Get SOPs (labor rates, markup, fees, gas). 3. Train knowledge base. 4. Build internal chatbot. 5. Employee inputs vehicle model, selects exact model, describes issue, gets instant quote. 6. Upsell: external customer-facing chatbot.

Tools Stack

CRM integration, AI voice calling platform, Parts supplier API connections, Internal chatbot builder, Knowledge base training tools. Specific platforms not named - developer team handles technical choices.

Client Acquisition

Partner Model: Partner with marketing agencies who have 20-30 clients with high lead volume. Offer 20-30% commission per referral ($2-3K per $10-15K deal). Stack 5-10 agency partners. Also paid ads ($10K/month).

Outreach Script

Speed-to-Lead: Your show-up rate is only 50% because you get to leads 20-30 minutes late. You spend $5-6K on this employee who has bad speed-to-lead. This agent scales with your business, fixes your speed-to-lead, and costs less. Quoting: What would you do with 2-3 extra hours daily if you were not sending free quotes?

Sales Process

1. Partner with marketing agencies (20-30% commission). 2. Agency refers clients. 3. Discovery call focused on pain. 4. Never sell features, sell pain. 5. Show ROI math vs employee costs. 6. Close on monthly or hybrid. 7. Hand off to developers. 8. Move to next sales call.

Objection Handling

Already have someone: They cost 5% commission, have terrible speed-to-lead, need constant retraining. Do not need AI receptionist: This is not inbound receptionist (saturated), this is outbound speed-to-lead. Too expensive upfront: Switch to monthly recurring, compare to employee cost.

Contrarian Insight

Inbound AI receptionists are saturated - go opposite with outbound agents. If everyone on Instagram posts about Claude systems, that route will not make money. Never sell on features, sell on pain. 100% profit margin doing everything yourself makes LESS money than 75% with a team (2 clients/month vs 10). The quoting agent (internal, not customer-facing) is top seller.

Mistake Warning

Doing everything yourself limits to 2 clients/month. Selling inbound receptionists in saturated market. Selling on features instead of pain. Following where market is headed instead of going opposite.

Scalability

Partner model with marketing agencies -> First clients via referral commissions -> Hire US developers for 20% -> Owner focuses 100% on sales -> 5 speed-to-lead clients = $10K MRR -> 50 clients = $1M+ recurring -> Add quoting agent upsells at $12-20K -> Hire more developers as volume grows -> $85K+/month.

Best Quotes

It is 99% dominated by technical people who are really good on the back end but cannot sell.
If you can only take on two clients a month at 10K versus me taking on 10, that is how I have $80-100K months.
I get zero dopamine from sitting in back-end systems. I am not good at it.
Wherever the market is headed, head the other way.

Action Steps

  • Build an outbound speed-to-lead agent demo that calls within 20 seconds
  • Identify 5-10 marketing agencies and pitch the partner model at 20-30% commission
  • Find and vet 2-3 US-based AI developers for 20% of deal revenue
  • Build a quoting agent demo for mobile mechanics to show live in sales calls
  • Structure speed-to-lead deals as monthly recurring $2-3K to build predictable revenue

Full Description

Tutorial on building micro SaaS products using Base44, an AI app builder. Micro SaaS = small, focused software solving one clear problem for one specific niche. Not trying to build the next billion-dollar unicorn. Examples: Bank Statement Converter ($16K/month), Screenshot One ($12K/month), Gift My Book (7-figure ARR). Framework covers idea validation through audience building, acute pain point identification, building minimal solution, generating word of mouth, and reinvesting in growth. Includes market gap heat map for finding underserved niches.

Pain Point

Most people cant build SaaS because they cant code and cant afford developers. Even with AI, most vibe-coded products get stuck at $10 MRR because the core idea is wrong, not the execution. The hardest part isnt building anymore - its choosing the right problem.

Pricing Justification

People dont pay for beautiful dashboards - they pay for relief. Save time, reduce mistakes, or make money easier to manage.

Delivery Process

1. Build audience first (50 engaged people enough). 2. Identify acute pain points from audience (not nice-to-have, need-this-fixed-NOW problems). 3. Build minimal solution - one job, one wow moment. 4. Generate word of mouth (one-click invites, shareable results). 5. Reinvest in audience growth with testimonials and proof.

Tools Stack

Base44 (AI app builder - primary, builds full SaaS from plain English descriptions), handles authentication, databases, email flows, payment integrations automatically. No coding required.

Sales Process

1. Prospect searches Google for solution to specific problem. 2. Lands on your tool page with clear promise. 3. Tries it - experiences wow moment within minutes. 4. Low friction trial → paid conversion. 5. Word of mouth spreads tool to others.

Objection Handling

People will happily compliment your idea but compliments dont build businesses - payments do. People tell the truth with their wallets. Test with payment, not praise.

Contrarian Insight

Ideas dont win - markets win. A genius idea in a weak market struggles. A simple idea in the right market prints. Large SaaS companies focus on massive industries, leaving smaller specific niches underserved. Build for overlooked groups where competition drops and willingness to pay goes up, especially in industries that look boring.

Mistake Warning

Picking the right idea in the wrong niche, or building before listening. Most beginners think the first step is building - its not. You win by listening first. Building without signal is just guessing, and guessing is expensive.

Scalability

Flywheel: audience → feedback → better features → better results → more word of mouth → more audience. Each cycle spins faster. Can reach $10K-$50K/month in right niche with focused funnel.

Best Quotes

People dont pay for beautiful dashboards, they pay for relief.
People will happily compliment your idea. But compliments dont build businesses. Payments do. People tell the truth with their wallets.

Action Steps

  • Use Base44 to build and launch a micro SaaS in a weekend - describe the tool in plain English and iterate
  • Start with 50 engaged people in a niche community, listen to their pain points, then build for their specific need

  • Validate with payments not praise - charge from day one to test real demand

Nick Saraev's step-by-step playbook for AI consulting from $0 to $25K/month. Five pillars: Marketing, Sales, Onboarding, Fulfillment, Retention. Scaled multiple agencies to $160K/month combined. Provides free templates, scripts, and workflows.

Pain Point

Businesses have money for AI but don't know how to implement it. Need specific, measurable results not vague promises.

Pricing Justification

Helped supplement company add $32K revenue in one month. Value-based: charge based on dollars saved or revenue generated, not hours worked.

Delivery Process

1. Marketing: Upwork proposals, cold email, community presence, cold DMs 2. Sales: rapport > set schedule > discovery > solutions > investment > proposal 3. Onboarding: structured intake process 4. Fulfillment: build and deploy automations 5. Retention: ongoing optimization and expansion

Tools Stack

Upwork (platform sales), Instantly (cold email), LinkedIn/Instagram/X (cold DMs), Maker School (community). Cold email infrastructure for scale.

Client Acquisition

Four methods: 1) Upwork (highest conversion, least scalable) 2) Cold email (most scalable, highest barrier) 3) Communities (slow but relationship-rich) 4) Cold DMs (high time, high potential)

Outreach Script

Cold DM example: 'Hey Sarah, I've been seeing XYZ Wellness everywhere recently. Huge work. I help health companies automate their onboarding and reduce churn with a $15,000 a month minimum guarantee. Sales Process

1. Build rapport (get them to smile) 2. Set the schedule ('Here's how these calls usually go...') 3. Discovery ('Why did you decide to book this call?' - highest ROI question ever) 4. Solutions (match their pain to your offer) 5. Investment (present pricing) 6. Send proposal

Objection Handling

Contrarian Insight

'Why did you decide to book the call?' is probably the highest ROI question ever and basically nobody asks it. First cold email campaign will be your worst - have runway to persist. Communities are like fishing in Runescape - slow start, very valuable at high level. Simple is NOT easy.

Mistake Warning

Spending time on anything other than lead generation at $0 revenue. Procrastinating on marketing. First cold email campaign being your worst and giving up. Not having runway for cold email experimentation. Being too formal in cold DMs.

Scalability

Upwork first gigs -> Cold email infrastructure -> Community authority -> Cold DMs -> All four channels simultaneously -> Scale to $25K+/month

Best Quotes

If you're at zero in revenue and spending any time on anything not directly generating leads, you are wasting money
Sales today is not about tricking people. It is about uncovering their pain
Why did you decide to book the call? Probably the highest ROI question ever and basically nobody asks it
Your first campaign is probably going to be your worst

Action Steps

  • Set up Upwork profile with custom CV and video proposal template
  • Write one cold email script following: personalization, value prop, niche, goal, case study, cliffhanger, CTA
  • Join 2-3 communities and post value content 3x/week
  • Practice the 5-step sales structure on your next call
  • Send 10 cold DMs on LinkedIn this week with the Sarah template

Pain Point

Customer support teams overwhelmed with ticket volume. Need co-pilot to draft responses. Knowledge scattered across specialized human agents, Slack, YouTube videos. No centralized FAQ system. Manual response drafting is slow and inconsistent.

Pricing Justification

Eliminates or reduces need for multiple support agents. Consistent response quality. 24/7 availability. Auto-generated FAQs reduce future ticket volume.

Delivery Process

1. Create Zendesk webhook pointing to n8n 2. Create Zendesk trigger for new/updated tickets 3. n8n receives ticket ID via webhook 4. Two API calls: get ticket info + get all comments 5. Route: open tickets to response agent, solved tickets to FAQ generator 6. AI generates response using Perplexity API (restricted to client website) 7. Post response back to Zendesk (internal note or public reply) 8. Log everything to Google Sheets 9. Generate FAQs from solved tickets weekly

Tools Stack

n8n (workflow automation), Zendesk/Gorgeous/Freshdesk (CRM), Perplexity API (AI search restricted to client domain - replaces RAG/Pinecone), Google Sheets (logging and FAQ storage), OpenAI (response generation)

Client Acquisition

YouTube tutorials, Gumroad template sales, agency service offering

Sales Process

Not detailed - tutorial-focused content

Objection Handling

Safety concern: start with internal notes (co-pilot mode) before enabling direct customer replies. Accuracy: Perplexity API restricted to client website ensures responses based on actual product info.

Contrarian Insight

Don't build a RAG database with Pinecone - it's double-handling information that already exists on the client's website. Instead, use Perplexity API restricted to the website domain for live AI-powered search. Start with internal notes not direct replies - much safer. Use Google Sheets for logging every interaction instead of digging through the ticketing system. Auto-generate FAQs from solved tickets to build knowledge base organically.

Mistake Warning

Sending AI responses directly to customers before validating accuracy. Not logging interactions to a separate Google Sheet for easy review. Building RAG databases when the information already exists on the website. Not using different response styles for different channels (email vs SMS).

Scalability

Build template -> Sell on Gumroad -> Offer done-for-you setup -> Add ongoing monitoring retainer -> Expand to multiple CRM platforms -> Scale agency

Best Quotes

I'm not building a RAG database. I'm not using Pinecone. A lot of people have websites with heaps of pages and all the product information is on the website
We're using the Perplexity API search and restricting domains directly to the website
Initially I would just suggest responding back with internal notes because it's a lot safer
One thing I find is it's really good to build a Google Sheet where you can have every single interaction logged

Action Steps

  • Set up n8n with Zendesk webhook integration
  • Configure Perplexity API restricted to one client domain
  • Build the two-route workflow: responses for open tickets, FAQs for solved
  • Create Google Sheet logging template for all AI interactions
  • Start with internal notes only - review accuracy for 1 week before enabling direct replies

Remove quotation marks from question field (critical detail). 11. Add branching conditions: Booked (provided service need + address + preferred day), Emergency (urgent situation), Unqualified (outside area or irrelevant). 12. Set up SMS notifications for each branch. 13. Publish and save. 14. Duplicate workflow for dog fooding client acquisition.

Tools Stack

GoHighLevel ($97-$297/month - CRM, automation, AI conversation, SMS, workflows), Claude or ChatGPT (generating AI personality prompts and knowledge base), Outscraper.com (scraping business phone numbers - free tier), Slack Broadcast (ringless voicemail drop service), Voice Memos app (recording voicemail messages)

Client Acquisition

DOG FOODING: 1. Go to Outscraper.com. 2. Search target business type in target city (e.g. tree trimming Dallas Texas). 3. Download phone numbers as CSV (got 181 numbers). 4. Clean numbers (remove +1 space, dashes). 5. Record vague ringless voicemail incentivizing callback. 6. Upload to Slack Broadcast for ringless voicemail drops. 7. When business owners call back, GHL triggers AI text-back that sells them the service.

Outreach Script

Ringless voicemail: 'Hey, this is Chris Kerner. I'm here in Dallas, Texas, and I'm looking for a tree trimming business. So, please give me a call back. Thanks.' AI sales text: 'Hey, I know this is random, but I'm using a missed call text back on you right now that will have a full conversation with you with AI. It feels real, doesn't it? I want to install it for you on your tree trimming business so you can close more jobs. What questions do you have?' Price response: 'Great question. It is $500 a month, but if it doesn't pay for itself in the first 90 days, then I'll give you all your money back. Can we hop on a call so I can explain? It'll only take a minute.'

Sales Process

1. Scrape phone numbers for target business type via Outscraper. 2. Record vague 10-second ringless voicemail. 3. Send RVM drops to entire list. 4. AI text-back system engages callers automatically. 5. AI qualifies interest (hot lead vs unqualified). 6. Business owner gets SMS with lead name and phone for hot leads. 7. Human follows up to close on a call.

Objection Handling

Price objection built into AI: '$500/month but if it doesn't pay for itself in the first 90 days, then it's free.' Risk-reversal close automated in the sales conversation.

Contrarian Insight

Dog fooding - use the exact same AI system you're selling to FIND and CLOSE clients. Every business has two functions: find customers and fulfill those customers. This does both with the same workflow. Also: a 16-year-old girl with no experience closed a plumber client using this approach, proving zero barrier to entry.

Mistake Warning

Leaving quotation marks in the AI question field (embarrassing when sent). Setting delay between AI responses too low (10 seconds feels robotic - use 35 seconds). Not adding branching conditions for emergency/unqualified which wastes time.

Scalability

Copy-paste workflow for every new client - just swap business-specific info. 33 million small business owners as addressable market. Dog fooding workflow generates leads passively through ringless voicemail drops. Community (Playmakers at playmakersai.com) for support.

Best Quotes

57% of sales goes to whoever responds first. And that's not my opinion. That's data.
Most small businesses are letting calls go to voicemail and hoping people call back. Side note, they don't.
This is a $500 to $2,000 a month per client service.
Every business has two functions. Find customers, fulfill those customers. We just did both with the same workflow.

Action Steps

  • Sign up for GoHighLevel 30-day free trial and build the missed call text back workflow following the exact steps
  • Go to Outscraper.com and scrape 100-200 phone numbers for ONE local service business type in your city
  • Record a vague 10-second ringless voicemail and send via Slack Broadcast
  • Duplicate the workflow for dog fooding - set up AI sales agent to pitch the service to inbound callers
  • Join Playmakers community at playmakersai.com for additional strategies

Full Description

TK Kader shares 3 AI SaaS ideas using his Unstoppable Ideas framework (Who, Why, What). Idea 1: Trained Negotiator - Chrome extension sitting inside Gmail that applies human psychology to read email threads and provides negotiation advice, draft responses, and insights on what the other party is really saying. Targets salespeople, HR, recruiters, PR, legal. Idea 2: Website Message Creator - helps founders constantly changing their website messaging by analyzing if their messaging resonates with target audience, combating AI-induced delusion where ChatGPT makes everything sound legit. Both priced at $30-99/month targeting commission-based roles.

Pain Point

Idea 1: People spend all day negotiating via email but manually copy-paste threads into Gemini for analysis - repetitive and inefficient. Idea 2: Founders are delusionally confident in messaging because ChatGPT validates everything. They need objective AI analysis of whether messaging actually resonates with their market.

Pricing Justification

People will pay for convenience AND expertise. Not every salesperson knows the right prompts. The custom alpha is specialized prompts plus workflow integration inside existing tools. If it helps them close one more deal, the ROI is obvious.

Delivery Process

1. Build Chrome extension for Gmail. 2. Apply trained negotiation prompts to email thread context. 3. Provide analysis of counterpartys position, unsaid cues, and recommended responses. 4. Integrate with CRM data for richer context. 5. Add chat interface for deeper conversation analysis.

Objection Handling

Why not just use ChatGPT/Gemini? Answer: People pay for convenience AND expertise. The specialized prompts and in-context workflow integration are the moat. Also connects to CRM data for richer analysis that manual prompting cant replicate.

Contrarian Insight

AI-induced delusion is a real problem. Founders get into long enough conversations with ChatGPT and everything it says seems totally legit, leading to delusional confidence in messaging that doesnt actually resonate with the market. The antidote is objective, data-driven messaging analysis.

Mistake Warning

The worst thing to do is waste months building the wrong product or building a product no one wants. Always validate the Who (ICP), Why (pain), and What (10x better solution) before building.

Scalability

Chrome extension → team licenses → enterprise. Start at $30/month individual → $99+/month with CRM integration → enterprise pricing for sales teams. Can expand from Gmail to Outlook and other platforms.

Best Quotes

People will pay for convenience, but more importantly, people will pay for expertise.
Theres this thing happening where you get into a long enough conversation with ChatGPT and anything it says to you just seems totally legit. You end up being completely delusional.
What makes for a great SaaS idea is not about vibe coding the product. Its the core kernel of the idea - whether it was a good idea in terms of the market youre going after.

Covers Core Four: warm outreach, cold outreach, content, paid ads. Includes verbatim script from Hormozi doing live warm outreach.

Pain Point

Relying only on grinding creates hamster wheel growth. No reviews/referral system means leaving money on table. Word of mouth is highest percentage of sales across any market but most businesses dont ask for reviews or referrals.

Pricing Justification

Hormozi gives away all courses and secrets for free → builds reciprocity → people buy whatever he offers because they feel they owe him.

Delivery Process

1. Start with warm outreach (call people you know). 2. Follow Hormozi script: introduction → ask about their life → ask if they have time for [your service] → ask if they know anyone else → offer free trial. 3. Track everything in CRM/Notion. 4. Build self-reinforcing system: deliver results → get reviews/referrals → more clients. 5. Add cold outreach, content, and paid ads as you scale.

Tools Stack

Notion (momentum operating system for tracking outreach and leads), Instantly (automated outbound email campaigns), CRM tools. Hormozi framework: Core Four (warm outreach, cold outreach, content, paid ads).

Client Acquisition

Hormozis Core Four: 1) Warm outreach (call people you know first), 2) Cold outreach, 3) Content creation, 4) Paid advertising. Start with warm outreach for immediate results.

Outreach Script

Hormozi verbatim warm outreach script: Hey whats up man, [ask about their life]. Quick question for you - is anyone [doing your service]? No? Got it. Do you know anybody else who also might not have [service]? What Im trying to do is Im looking for five or ten people to do my service for free just so I can show how good my service is. Ill text you with the details. The other folks you know, do you think you could do a three-way text intro with me?

Sales Process

1. Introduction (ask about their life). 2. Ask if they have the problem your service solves. 3. Ask for referrals (do you know anyone else?). 4. Offer free trial to build momentum. 5. Follow up and track in CRM. 6. Convert free trials to paid via demonstrated results.

Objection Handling

If nervous: follow a script. Hormozi provides exact word-for-word script. Free trial removes the money objection. Three-way text intro removes the I dont know anyone objection.

Contrarian Insight

You probably have gold sitting in your phone, Instagram followers, and LinkedIn connections right now. Most people rely on cold outreach when warm outreach can get a deal in minutes. Also: the harder I work the more my business grows is a hamster wheel - you need systems and automation, not just grinding. And: most people dont ask for reviews/referrals despite word of mouth being the highest percentage of sales.

Mistake Warning

Not building a self-reinforcing system. Just doing get the next deal, get the next deal without reviews/referrals/automation means youre always on a hamster wheel. Also: not tracking outreach in a CRM - spraying and praying without data means no learning.

Scalability

Warm outreach → free trials → results → reviews/referrals → self-reinforcing flywheel. Add cold outreach (Instantly email automation), content creation, and paid ads as momentum builds. Notion operating system tracks everything.

Best Quotes

The highest percentage of sales across any market come from word of mouth.
We want to create a system that is self-reinforcing that continues to grow and compound over time.
The rule of reciprocity: offer free stuff that others charge for so people feel like they owe you.
If youre not getting customers, dont blame the market. Point the finger at yourself.

Action Steps

  • Start with warm outreach today using Hormozis verbatim script - call people you already know
  • Offer free trial or discount as momentum campaign to get first clients and build proof
  • Build self-reinforcing system: results → reviews → referrals → more clients
  • Track everything in Notion or CRM - never spray and pray without data
  • Add Instantly for automated cold email outreach as second channel

Nick Saraev
7-Figure Pricing for Automation		 Automation Nick Saraev breaks down 8 pricing methods for automation agencies: hourly, cost- SMBs needing automations (10 staff), enterprise companies (1 Cost-saving example: $10-15K one-time (25% of $50K annual savings). Revenue uplift: 10% of revenue generated. Ongoing maintenance retainers after initial build. Subscription models for recurring value. $100K+ in one month. Scaled agency to $72K/mo Upwork ($500K+ earned), cold outreach, community b

Full Description

Nick Saraev breaks down 8 pricing methods for automation agencies: hourly, cost-saving, revenue uplift, flat project fee, retainer, subscription, hybrid, and value-based. Made $100K+ in one month using make.com. Scaled agency to $72K/month. Each method has specific use cases, pros, and cons. No pricing method is perfect - best agencies constantly re-evaluate.

Pain Point

Pricing is the biggest and hardest moving part of automation. Beginners undercharge because they price on time not value. The time spent building doesn't reflect the value delivered.

Pricing Justification

Cost-saving: if you eliminate a $50K/year employee role, charge $12.5K (25%). Revenue uplift: if cold email system generates $X, charge 10% of that. Value-based: if 1% margin improvement on $100M company = $1M, charge accordingly.

Delivery Process

1. Consult with client to understand pain points 2. Identify cost savings or revenue opportunities 3. Propose value-based pricing 4. Build automation using make.com/n8n 5. Deploy and test 6. Transition to maintenance retainer 7. Re-evaluate pricing as relationship evolves

Tools Stack

make.com (primary automation platform), Zapier, n8n. No-code/low-code automation tools.

Client Acquisition

Upwork ($500K+ earned), cold outreach, community building (Maker School)

Sales Process

1. Discover client pain points 2. Quantify cost savings or revenue opportunity 3. Propose pricing as percentage of value 4. Build and deliver 5. Transition to retainer

Objection Handling

Client worried about cost escalation with hourly: switch to flat fee. Client questioning value: quantify in dollars saved or revenue generated. Uncertainty about scope: start with hourly discovery then transition to value-based.

Contrarian Insight

No pricing method is perfect. Hourly penalizes you for being good (work faster = make less). Cost-saving is harder to justify than revenue uplift because saving money feels less exciting than making money. You can never charge the full amount you save - max about half due to risk and uncertainty. The time you spend building does NOT reflect value delivered. Template-based systems can make you $10K+ in a few hours.

Mistake Warning

Sticking with hourly pricing past first few gigs. Not talking about money with prospects (the elephant in the room). Quoting way too much or way too little from lack of experience. Not re-evaluating pricing as you grow.

Scalability

Hourly gigs -> Cost-saving projects -> Revenue uplift deals -> Retainer + subscription hybrid -> Enterprise value-based pricing -> Multiple pricing models for different client tiers

Best Quotes

The time that you spend building a system does not accurately reflect the value that you bring to an organization
If you are really really good you are going to work much faster and make less money because of it
You can make 10,000 bucks in a few hours if you build it right
No pricing method is perfect

Action Steps

  • Audit current pricing: are you charging hourly? Plan transition to value-based
  • For next prospect: quantify their cost savings AND revenue opportunity in dollars
  • Create pricing templates for cost-saving and revenue uplift proposals
  • Practice talking about money in discovery calls
  • Build one templated automation you can deploy in hours and charge $5-10K for

Traditional SMMA is dead - you are a glorified employee and slave for a couple thousand when you are better off just getting a job. The AI agency model gives the entire bridge while SMMA just gives a brick. Everyone reads the same Hormozi book and offers the same commoditized service.

Mistake Warning

Do not start with high-ticket online businesses if you have not run one before. Do not charge too little. The SMMA model takes 10x the effort for 1/5 the revenue.

Scalability

Start by learning skills at $97 -> Land first client at $5K -> Outsource everything -> Scale to $10K deals -> Build referral flywheel -> Add monthly recurring -> Graduate to equity/commission deals.

Best Quotes

Their traditional agency offer is almost like giving a business owner a brick. You are giving them a brick instead of a bridge.
You are essentially a glorified kind of employee and you are a little bit of a slave to an extent.
33% as in 2,855 people in this example were successful and actually turned into appointments.
Profit margins stay high at 70 to 80% while demand is obviously very high for this stuff.

Action Steps

  • Pick one niche from home services and build your first AI caller demo
  • Partner with a white-label ad agency for media buying
  • Find an AI developer who can build voice callers for $500-800 per project
  • Create a live demo dashboard with real call metrics for sales conversations
  • Start at $5K if not confident for $10K but always include the full bridge

Pricing Justification

Test in demo environment before going live.

Mistake Warning

Most people will not take action. The question is not whether this works, it is whether you will open Google Maps tonight.

Scalability

Best Quotes

You are not selling software, you are selling a missed revenue recovery system.

Scaled AI agency to six figures. 50+ business Cold calling best for receptionist. Approach busin

Full Description

Michele Torti breaks down 6 specific AI agency offers: (1) AI Inbound Receptionist - 24/7 voice agent answering calls, booking appointments, filtering spam; (2) Lead Reactivation - AI re-engages cold leads in CRMs via phone/SMS/email with performance-based pricing; (3) Speed to Lead - automated AI callback within 5 minutes of form fill; (4) Content Dashboard - automated content generation in Airtable/Sheets for LinkedIn, Instagram, Facebook, blogs; (5) Hiring Systems - automated applicant screening and candidate tracking; (6) AI UGC Creatives - AI-generated UGC video ads for e-commerce at scale using Sora and N8N.

Pain Point

Missed calls costing $1,500-$5,000 per lead. Cold leads in CRM collecting dust. Slow follow-up killing conversion (100x more likely to convert within 5 min). Content inconsistency. Manual hiring admin. E-commerce paying $200-$1,000 per UGC video.

Pricing Justification

Receptionist: 5 missed calls/week at med spa = $8K/month lost. Charge 20-25%. Speed to lead: 100x conversion in 5 min vs 1 hour. Reactivation: leads already paid for. UGC: replacing $200-$1K creators with $100-$300 AI.

Delivery Process

1. Choose one offer. 2. Identify niche with ROI math. 3. Build with N8N/Make + Retell + Twilio. 4. Receptionist: 24/7 voice, book appointments, FAQs, transfers, spam filter. 5. Reactivation: import CRM, configure outreach. 6. Speed to lead: integrate ads/CRM, trigger 5-min callback. 7. Content: Airtable/Sheets + OpenAI/Claude + scheduling. 8. Hiring: ATS to ClickUp/Notion with screening. 9. UGC: Sora + N8N pipeline. 10. Price per appointment or setup + retainer.

Tools Stack

N8N - automations $5/mo. Retell AI - voice (client pays). Twilio - phone (client pays). OpenAI/Claude - content. Sheets/Airtable - dashboard. Buffer/Hootsuite - scheduling. ClickUp/Notion - tracking. Sora - UGC video. Make.com - alt automation. GHL/HubSpot - CRM. Indeed/LinkedIn - ATS.

Client Acquisition

Cold calling best for receptionist. Approach businesses running ads 6+ months for reactivation. Show 100x conversion study for speed to lead. Build free mockup before pitching.

Outreach Script

References separate cold calling video for receptionist offer. Not provided verbatim here.

Sales Process

1. Identify businesses with pain point. 2. Frame ROI (makes or saves money). 3. Be specific on problem/audience/method. 4. Show mathematical ROI proof. 5. Risk-free performance pricing for reactivation. 6. Close on setup + retainer.

Objection Handling

Performance-based removes risk. Per booked appointment not per close. 5-minute study as proof for speed to lead. Avoid healthcare for content compliance.

Contrarian Insight

Most fail saying 'I do AI automation services' - feature not outcome. Must have clear tangible ROI owners can calculate. Being everything to everyone = nothing to no one. More specific = easier sell + higher price. Per appointment NOT per close.

Mistake Warning

Selling features not outcomes. Not specific enough. Healthcare content (compliance). Performance + setup fee simultaneously as beginner. Slow follow-up.

Scalability

Start with receptionist. Productize content dashboards. Stack offers per client. UGC scales with batch. Hiring creates sticky retainers. Move to monthly retainers.

Best Quotes

It either has to make the business money, or it has to save the business money. That is the filter.
Being everything to everyone means you are being nothing to no one.
One missed call for a roofing company could be a $3,000 job. For a med spa, it can be a $5,000 job.
If you follow up with a lead within 5 minutes, they are 100 times more likely to convert compared to following up an hour later.

Action Steps

  • Week 1: Pick ONE offer (AI receptionist). Self-host N8N. Build demo with Retell + Twilio.
  • Week 2: Calculate ROI for 3 niches (med spas, HVAC, dental). Create one-page pitch.
  • Week 3: Cold call 50 businesses. Track niche response rates.
  • Week 4: Close first client. Document results. Build lead reactivation as upsell.

How to Price AI Workflows - Complete Framework		 Consulting Comprehensive pricing guide for AI workflow and automation services. Teaches val Businesses needing AI workflow automation - any business wit Value-based: 10% of annual savings (e.g., $1,200 for $12K annual savings). Starting point for negotiation. $1,500 to $15,000+ per month for ongoing retainers, 3-12 month terms Has tried every pricing model (fixed fee, hou Not detailed in this video - focused on pricing st

Full Description

Comprehensive pricing guide for AI workflow and automation services. Teaches value-based pricing (charge based on business impact, not hours) combined with monthly retainers. Key formula: if workflow saves $12K/year, charge 10% ($1,200) for 10x ROI in first year. Two recommended models: 1) Value-based pricing for initial projects (foot in the door, builds trust), 2) Monthly retainers for ongoing work ($1,500-$15,000/month, 3-12 month terms). Milestone-based retainers position you as consultant vs hourly freelancer. Goal: work toward chief AI officer role within client organizations.

Pain Point

Businesses dont pay for time, they pay for outcomes. Most AI automation providers price on time/effort instead of value delivered. No industry standard pricing exists yet for AI automation services. Businesses need enablement, audits, and education but dont know what AI can do for them.

Pricing Justification

10x ROI rule: client should see 10x return on investment in first year. Walk through math step-by-step to build trust. Example: process costs 1hr/day × $50/hr × 250 days = $12K/year savings. 10% = $1,200 starting price. Plus opportunity cost of freed employee time on higher-value work.

Delivery Process

1. Discovery phase: map entire manual process (frequency, triggers, stakeholders, time per occurrence, hourly value, employees involved). 2. Compare manual vs automated version. 3. Calculate ROI for the automated portion only (dont overpromise). 4. Present value-based price with step-by-step math. 5. Deliver project and build trust. 6. Transition to monthly retainer for ongoing work. 7. Iterate and scale impact over time.

Tools Stack

AI automation/workflow tools (not specified by name), discovery frameworks for mapping manual processes. Emphasizes the consulting methodology over specific tools.

Client Acquisition

Not detailed in this video - focused on pricing strategy

Sales Process

1. Discovery: map full manual process with metrics. 2. Calculate ROI of automation. 3. Present price with transparent math (be ready to explain how you got the number). 4. Close on value-based project. 5. Deliver and build trust. 6. Transition to monthly retainer.

Objection Handling

When client asks how did you get that number - walk through math step-by-step. This builds instant trust. Anchor price on real metrics so it feels like an investment not an expense. Also: ROI compounds over time (month 1: save $1K, month 2: $1.5K, month 3: $2K) because freed time enables higher-value work.

Contrarian Insight

There is currently NO industry standard for pricing AI automation services. Nobody has agreed on the right way to price workflows. This is both a challenge and an opportunity. Also: youd much rather work with one $15K/month client than five $3K/month clients even though total revenue is same - less chaos, deeper relationship, better results, more referrals. Hours-based pricing makes you look like a freelancer; milestone-based positions you as consultant.

Mistake Warning

Pricing based on time/effort instead of outcomes. Also: over-promising results - if you only automate half the process, factor in that half not the full process into your ROI calculation. And: trying to maximize value extraction from each individual project inside a retainer instead of stacking wins and building trust for higher retainers over time.

Scalability

Value-based projects → monthly retainers ($1.5K-$15K/month) → performance bonuses tied to key metrics → chief AI officer positioning within client organizations. Higher retainers over time as impact scales. One deep client relationship beats five shallow ones.

Best Quotes

Businesses dont pay for your time, they pay for outcomes.
There is no industry standard way to price AI automation services yet. Nobody has agreed on the right way.
You should be able to show them exactly how you got to that number. Assume after you tell them the price theyre going to say can you tell me exactly how you got there.
Youd much rather work with one $15,000 per month client than five $3,000 per month clients. Less chaos, less stress, and you get to build a real relationship.

Action Steps

  • Use value-based pricing: charge 10% of first-year savings as starting price point
  • During discovery, map the full manual process with specific metrics (frequency, time, hourly cost, employee count)
  • Walk clients through ROI math step-by-step to build trust and justify pricing
  • Start with value-based projects then transition to $1.5K-$15K/month retainers as trust builds
  • Position as AI consultant/partner not freelancer - use milestone-based deliverables not hourly billing

Small businesses don't have websites and are invisible online. Missing digital presence = missing customers. Don't know what their business could look like online.

Free demo - zero risk for prospect. Website is already built before the call. They just need to say yes and send email to receive it.

Send demo link to interested leads 8. Close on website purchase

Tools Stack

Vapi (AI voice agent platform), make.com (automation), Google Sheets (lead list + CRM), ChatGPT (prompt writing + knowledge base generation), OpenAI model (Vapi backend), Deepgram (transcription), Google Maps scraper

Client Acquisition

Fully automated AI cold calling from scraped Google Maps data. Zero manual outreach needed.

Outreach Script

AI voice script: 'Hey there. How's your day going? [pause for answer] My name's Alex. I work with a local company called Memberify. We build websites for small businesses. I noticed your business doesn't have a website, so I went ahead and built one for you. No charge or anything. Just wanted to show you what your business could look like online. It's already done. Do you mind if I email you the link real quick so you can take a look?'

Sales Process

1. AI calls from Google Sheet automatically 2. Friendly opener (How's your day?) 3. Pitch: pre-built website already done for them 4. Handle objections conversationally 5. Collect name and email 6. Send demo link 7. Follow up for purchase decision

Objection Handling

Is it free? 'Yep. The preview is totally free. If you like it and want to use it, we can talk about that later.' Hesitant: 'A lot of small businesses in the area don't have a website. We figured it'd be more helpful to show you a finished preview instead of cold pitching.' Not interested: move on to next lead automatically.

Contrarian Insight

Don't start the AI call with a pitch - say 'how's your day going' and wait for response. Leave the 'first message' field empty in Vapi so the bot doesn't speak until the human says hello. Enable end-call function or the bot gets stuck in an infinite goodbye loop. Let ChatGPT write your Vapi prompt (meta: AI writing prompt for AI). Use Perplexity-style knowledge base from your website instead of manual documentation.

Mistake Warning

Having a first message that bombards the lead immediately. Not enabling end-call function (infinite goodbye loop). Making AI responses too long and formal. Not testing the agent yourself before deploying. Forgetting to set up the make.com outcome retrieval automation.

Scalability

Scrape 50 leads -> Build Vapi agent -> Automate calling via make.com -> Sell websites at $1,500-$2,000 -> Scale to hundreds of calls/day -> Add more services beyond websites

Best Quotes

I noticed your business doesn't have a website so I went ahead and built one for you, no charge or anything
If you leave the first message empty, the assistant won't say anything until the lead says hello, which is much more natural
If we didn't have end call on, at the end of the call the bot would get stuck in this loop saying have a great day over and over
We're going to let AI write the prompt for our AI agent. That's fun

Action Steps

  • Sign up for Vapi and create your first voice agent
  • Scrape 50 local businesses without websites from Google Maps
  • Build make.com automation connecting Google Sheet to Vapi for auto-calling
  • Test the agent by calling yourself first
  • Build second make.com automation to retrieve and log call outcomes

n8n, Make.com, Late/Later (scheduling), Nano Banana Pro (AI visuals), AI voice platforms, SMS/email platforms, CRM integration, Instagram/Facebook Graph API

Client Acquisition

Free School community with 4-week challenge. Pain-point positioning. Referrals from real estate offices. Revenue share with content creators.

Outreach Script

Do not lead with I build AI automation. Lead with I help personal injury lawyers respond faster to leads so they do not lose $400,000 every year to their competition.

Sales Process

1. Identify industry and pain point. 2. Lead with dollar impact. 3. Explain simple fix. 4. Show ROI math. 5. Offer pricing model. 6. Start with one service as foot-in-the-door. 7. Upsell additional services.

Objection Handling

Frame against cost of NOT doing it. Revenue share option removes all risk.

Contrarian Insight

Database reactivation closes highest ticket deals but most builders overlook it. Revenue share with content creators is a gold mine most miss - leverage someone else s audience instead of finding clients from scratch. The hard part is not technology, it is understanding business problems.

Mistake Warning

Pricing based on time to build instead of value created. Leading with technology instead of business problem.

Scalability

Start with one service -> Land first client -> Upsell additional services -> Add revenue share deals with creators -> Stack 4+ industries -> Revenue share creates passive income layer.

Best Quotes

I built an outbound AI voice agent that generated an additional $118,000.
Every time he uses the system with one of his clients, I get 20%. He does 40 packages per month, I make $4,000.
Database reactivation consistently closes at the highest ticket rate.
Do not lead with I build AI automation. Lead with I help personal injury lawyers respond faster.

Action Steps

  • Pick ONE service to start - database reactivation has highest ticket
  • Build a revenue share deal with a content creator or coach
  • Create ROI calculators for each target industry
  • Reframe all pitches around the dollar cost of the problem
  • Use database reactivation as foot-in-the-door then upsell

Charlie Barber
5 AI Services To Sell For 5000 in 2026		 Automation Charlie Barber outlines 5 proven AI services: (1) AI Lead Reactivation and Outre Service 1: Med spas, coaches, info products. Service 2: Real A few thousand dollars per service. Low barrier to entry for reactivation. Content strategist replacement: 2K/month. Retention: ongoing. Sales analysis: weekly reports. 5000 new Instagram followers/month. Pays 2K/m Cold email campaigns. School.com outreach with Loo

Full Description

Charlie Barber outlines 5 proven AI services: (1) AI Lead Reactivation and Outreach - automated DM/email to old leads using MessengerFlow, AutoLeads, Instantly.ai; (2) AI Content Operator - avatar content with HeyGen + Descript + ManyChat + trial reels for 5000 new followers/month; (3) RevOps - connecting marketing/sales/fulfillment into one AI pipeline, diagnosing 3 leak points (lead-to-booking, call-to-close, close-to-onboarding); (4) SaaS Retention and Churn Reduction - automated follow-up messaging to increase LTV; (5) AI Sales and Conversion Agent - AI reviews sales calls/DMs/emails to identify objections and patterns, improving close rates 10%+.

Pain Point

Missing 30K-100K/year from unfollowed leads. Content inconsistency. Revenue leaking between stages. Customer churn. 10% close rate improvement at 10K/deal = 100K extra.

Pricing Justification

Reactivation: 30-100K unused in old leads. Content: replaces 2K/month human. RevOps: fewer leaks = more revenue. Retention: increases LTGP. Sales: 10% on 100 deals at 10K = 100K extra.

Delivery Process

Outreach: 1. Set up MessengerFlow/AutoLeads/Instantly. 2. Import old leads from CRM. 3. Create attractive offer. 4. Launch automated outreach. 5. AI responds via GHL. 6. Book appointments. Content: 1. LA10 for research. 2. Script with AI. 3. Clone owner with HeyGen. 4. Edit with Descript. 5. Test 5 hooks on trial reels. 6. Post winner to main. 7. ManyChat comment automation. 8. Notion pipeline. RevOps: 1. Diagnostic call. 2. ID worst leak. 3. Build automation. Sales: 1. Connect Fireflies. 2. N8N + LLM analysis. 3. Summarize calls. 4. Weekly reports + Slack recaps.

Tools Stack

MessengerFlow - Facebook DMs. AutoLeads.app - Instagram outreach. Instantly.ai - cold email. LA10 - content research. HeyGen - avatar cloning. Descript - AI video editing. ManyChat - comment automation. GoHighLevel - CRM/AI responses. Notion - pipeline. N8N - automation backbone. Fireflies - call recording. Slack - recaps. Claude/GPT - analysis. School.com - prospecting.

Client Acquisition

Cold email campaigns. School.com outreach with Loom VSSL (few minutes). Funnel: outreach > Loom > booked call > close.

Outreach Script

Framework: reach out on School.com, send short Loom VSSL focused on end results not mechanisms. Always sell the destination not the journey.

Sales Process

1. Outreach on School.com or cold email. 2. Short Loom VSSL on end results. 3. Funnel to booked call. 4. Act as doctor - diagnostic questions. 5. ID specific problem. 6. Mold offer to their problems. 7. Present ROI-focused solution. 8. Close.

Objection Handling

Act as doctor - morally wrong to prescribe without diagnosing. Ask questions first. Lead with value. Sell destination not journey. Show personal proof (I pay 2K/month for this myself).

Contrarian Insight

RevOps is SaaS term moving downstream - get on it early. Do not need Meta ads (high learning curve) - outreach is lower barrier. AI replaces 2K/month content strategists. Trial reels strategy: test 5 hooks on trial reels (new audience), post winner to main profile. You must look the part - optimize outreach profile for target niche.

Mistake Warning

Not knowing niche deeply enough. Not looking the part on profiles. Selling mechanisms instead of outcomes. Trying everything instead of diagnosing ONE leak. Not testing hooks before posting.

Scalability

Start reactivation (lowest barrier). Build to RevOps. Add content operator. Layer sales analysis for high-ticket. Each service compounds.

Best Quotes

Most businesses are missing out on 30 to 100K per year just because they are not following up with past customers.
Always sell the destination, not the journey.
Be just like a doctor. Get on a sales call. Ask questions. Mold yourself to their problems. Lead with value. That is how you build a real business.
A 10% improvement in close rate on 100 deals per year at 10K each can mean 100K in extra revenue.

Action Steps

  • Week 1: Choose reactivation or RevOps. Set up MessengerFlow + AutoLeads + Instantly. Create optimized outreach profile.
  • Week 2: Cold email 100 businesses. Send Loom VSSL under 3 minutes focused on results. Track responses.
  • Week 3: Diagnostic calls. ID leak points. Build first automation with N8N/GHL. Document results.
  • Week 4: Add content operator. Set up HeyGen + Descript + ManyChat. Test trial reels for one client.
  • Week 5: Layer sales analysis for high-ticket clients. Connect Fireflies + N8N + LLM for automated call analysis.
